--- Page 65 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological influences on agriculture, specifically focusing on the transit of the Sun through Scorpio and Taurus and the resulting "yogas" (planetary combinations) that predict crop failure or success.</meta>

->Page Signature: Ra<-

The unaligned <note>original: "alinga," referring to a planet without specific dignity or a specific astrological condition</note> planet causes destruction. A malefic planet <note>original: "papa," referring to naturally "evil" planets like Mars or Saturn</note> situated in the seventh house destroys the crops as soon as they are born. When the Sun is in Scorpio, positioned between the two malefic planets, Saturn and Mars—meaning one is in the second house and the other in the twelfth from the Sun—then it destroys the grain. Likewise, if a malefic planet, either Mars or Saturn, is situated in the seventh house from Scorpio, it destroys the grain even after it has been produced. Thus says the sage Badarayana <note>An ancient authority on Vedic astrology and philosophy</note>:

> The Sun, situated in Scorpio and placed between cruel planets, destroys the grain. A malefic planet situated in the seventh house also destroys the crops as they are born.

### Now, another combination is described:

If a cruel planet is in the house of wealth <note>the 2nd house</note> and is not aspected <note>viewed</note> by benefics, it destroys the first-born crops, though it may clearly allow for later production. If a cruel malefic planet, either Mars or Saturn, is in the second house from Scorpio and is not seen by benefics—meaning it is not aspected by Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus—it destroys the previously produced grain. However, it may allow the crops that are produced later to succeed.

### Now, another combination is described:

Two cruel planets situated in the "Jamitra" <note>the 7th house</note> and the "Kendra" <note>the 1st, 4th, or 10th houses</note> destroy the grain. If the two malefic planets, Mars and Saturn, are such that one is in the seventh house and the other is in a center—meaning either the fourth or the tenth house—then Mars and Saturn cause the destruction of the grain. If those same two cruel planets are aspected by benefic planets, they do not cause calamity everywhere in every country, but only in some places. Now, one might ask: since the "Chakra-Kendra" yoga already includes the seventh house, why specify the "Jamitra" (7th house) separately? It is mentioned to establish that if one planet is necessarily in the seventh and the other is in a center, then this specific yoga occurs. Thus says Badarayana:

> A malefic in the seventh house causes some harm, and one in a center also causes loss; but if they are aspected by benefic planets, one should not predict total destruction.

### Now, another combination is described:

When there are cruel planets in the seventh and sixth houses from the Sun situated in Scorpio, then there is a failure of crops and a "testing of prices" <note>original: "arghaparikshani," implying high prices or scarcity</note>. When the two malefics, Mars and Saturn, are in the seventh and sixth houses from the Sun in Scorpio—one in the seventh and the other in the sixth—then the crops fail to produce. Furthermore, there is a "testing of prices," meaning there is a scarcity and high prices, and little is obtained.

### Now, the rule for the autumn crops is given:

The destruction or success of the crops produced during the Sun's entry into Taurus should be understood by the same rule. By this same method specified for the Sun's entry into Scorpio, the wise experts in "Sasyajataka" <note>Agricultural Astrology</note> should determine the destruction, lack thereof, or the prosperity and success of the autumn crops when the Sun enters Taurus.

...should be known and understood. And so says Badarayana <note>A revered ancient sage often cited in astrological and philosophical texts</note>: "Thus are the planetary combinations described; when the Sun resides in Scorpio or Taurus, the autumn crops must be evaluated according to the reality of the situation." Now, by observing the Sun's movement, the scarcity or abundance of goods is explained in the following verse: When the Sun moves through the three signs starting with Aries, joined or aspected <note>term: aspected (drishta) — when one planet 'looks at' or influences another from a specific distance</note> by "Son of the Moon" <note>original: "saumyaputro" — usually refers to Mercury, but here the commentary expands this to include all benefic planets</note>, it produces a triple yield of grain, making it affordable and beneficial for all.

The Sun (Aditya), while staying in the three signs of Aries, Taurus, and Gemini, if joined with or seen by "Saumya" or benefic planets—namely Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus—creates a connection that results in "Samarghatva" <note>term: Samarghatva — low cost or affordable prices</note> and "Abhayopayoga" <note>original: "abhayopayogam" — interpreted here as being useful for safety or general welfare</note>. This means the goods are useful for both this world and the next; in this world for one's kinsmen, and for the sake of religious merit in the next.

Now, the condition of the autumn crops is discussed: When the Sun is situated in the signs of the Bow (Sagittarius), the Deer (Capricorn), and the Pot (Aquarius), the results for the autumn crops are the same as previously described, provided the timing is right; however, the opposite occurs if there is a connection with malefic planets.

The Sun, when staying in Sagittarius, Capricorn, or Aquarius, should be understood to affect autumn crops in the same way as described for the three signs starting with Aries. That is, if the Sun is joined or aspected by benefics, the autumn grain becomes affordable and useful. However, at the time of harvest, if there is a "Viparyaya" <note>term: Viparyaya — reversal or contrary result</note> due to the influence of "Krura" or malefic planets (such as Mars and Saturn), the opposite occurs. This means that if the Sun in these signs is joined or seen by malefics, there will be "Maharghata" <note>term: Maharghata — high prices or dearness</note> and fear. In such a contrary combination, one should sell the harvest at a high price.

### Thus ends the thirty-eighth chapter, named "The Nativity of Crops," in the Commentary on the Samhita composed by Bhattotpala.

<margin>133</margin>

### Now begins the chapter on the "Determination of Commodities."

To show the traditional authority <note>original: "agama" — sacred tradition or source texts</note> on this subject, the author says:

> I shall declare, according to the sacred tradition, which signs have been designated by the Sages as the lords of which commodities, for the purpose of determining their good or bad fortune (price).

Whichever signs (Aries, etc.) have been declared by Sages such as Kashyapa to be the lords of specific commodities, I shall describe them based on the tradition to understand their "Ardhata" <note>value or price fluctuations</note>. First, he states which goods belong to Aries: Woolen cloth, blankets, gold, grain, gems, and barley; as well as medicines produced on land...

Aries is declared to be the first of the stationary wheel. || Clothing and ornaments are well-known; "of sheep" <note>original: "avikām"</note> means that which is produced from them, such as wool. <insert>Woven blankets made from goat hair</insert>, wheat is well-known. Resin from the hollows of trees <note>likely referring to aromatic resins or gums</note>, barley is well-known. For all of these, and for the medicinal herbs produced on land—those herbs that grow upon the earth in both dry and wet places—as well as for gold, Aries is said to be the presiding sign. And so says the sage Kashyapa: "In Aries, gold, land-grown products, wheat, goats, and sheep are found; when in a favorable conjunction with gold, they bring auspicious results."

### || Now, regarding Taurus and Gemini ||

In Taurus <note>the Bull</note>, there are clothes, flowers, wheat, rice, barley, buffaloes, and the offspring of donkeys <note>mules</note>. In Gemini, there are grains, autumn-harvested crops, water-lily roots, and cotton. ||

Clothing, ornaments, and flowers are well-known. Wheat, rice (such as the sixty-day variety), and barley. Buffaloes and "offspring of donkeys" refer to bullocks or beasts of burden. These are associated with Taurus. In Gemini, "grains" are well-known; "autumnal" refers to those produced in the autumn season. Also, "creepers" <note>valli</note> such as climbing plants, "water-lily roots" <note>the edible tuber of the lotus</note>, and cotton are well-known. All these belong to Gemini. || And so says Kashyapa: "In Taurus are buffaloes, cattle, clothing, and rice produced in the Pushya constellation. In Gemini are grains, water-lily roots, creepers, cotton, and autumn crops."

### Now, regarding Cancer and Leo ||

In Cancer, there are kodo millet, plantains, durva grass, fruits, tubers, leaves, and cows. In Leo, there are grains, flavorful liquids, and the skins of lions and similar beasts, along with jaggery <note>unrefined sugar</note>. || Kodo millet is well-known. Plantains (bananas), durva grass, and all fruits such as nutmeg and others. Tubers are well-known as the roots or seeds found underground. Leaves refer to fragrant leaves. "Cows" include cattle or perhaps coconuts <note>the commentator offers an alternative reading of 'narikela'</note>. All these are in Cancer. In Leo, grains are well-known, specifically rice. "Flavorful liquids" <term>rasas: the six tastes—sweet, sour, salty, bitter, pungent, and astringent</term>. "Skins of lions and others" refers to the variegated hides of animals like lions, leopards, and cats. These, along with jaggery, are all in Leo. || And so says Kashyapa: "In Cancer are fruits, durva grass, kodo millet, and plantains. In Leo are grains, all flavorful liquids, the skins of lions and others, and jaggery."

### || Now, regarding Virgo and Libra ||

Sixty-day rice, oil, peas, horse-gram, wheat, mung beans, and flat beans. In the seventh sign <note>Libra</note>, there are black gram, barley, wheat, and mustard seeds. || [The first group] is well-known. Peas are a crop; horse-gram, wheat, and mung beans are all well-known. Those which grow again after being harvested are "flat beans" <note>nishpava</note>; some say these are a type of rice, others say they are pod-bearing legumes. These belong to Virgo. Black gram, barley, and wheat—what kind? "With mustard," meaning accompanied by mustard seeds. These are in the seventh sign, Libra. || And so says Kashyapa: "In Virgo are mung beans, wild rice, fruits, peas, and barley. In Libra are barley, wheat, black gram, and white mustard."

|| Now, regarding Scorpio... <note>The text cuts off as it prepares to discuss the next sign</note>

# Now he speaks of Sagittarius:

In the eighth sign <note>Scorpio</note>, liquids <note>original: "seka," referring to fluids or irrigated products</note>, iron, or wool are mentioned. In the ninth <note>Sagittarius</note>, horses, salt, clothing, weapons, sesame, grain, and threads. "Liquids" refers to well-known fluids or products of creepers and vines arising from irrigation. "Iron" refers to iron itself or bronze. "Goat" and "sheep" are well-known, and "wool" is that which is produced from them; all these are in the eighth sign. In the ninth, "horses" are steeds; "salt" is rock-salt; "clothing" is garments; "weapons" are arms such as bows and arrows; sesame, grain, and threads are well-known. These belong to the ninth sign, Sagittarius. And so says **Kashyapa** <note>An ancient sage and authority on Vedic astrology</note>: "In Scorpio: liquids, iron, and bronze. In Sagittarius: grain, weapons, salt, and the like."

# Now he speaks of Capricorn and Aquarius:

In Capricorn: trees, shrubs and the like, liquids, gold, and iron. In Aquarius: aquatic products, fruits, gems, and diverse forms. "Trees" and "shrubs" are well-known, meaning thickets like the *Arka* plant. "And the like" refers to creepers and vines. "Liquids" are those arising from irrigation. "Gold" is bright wealth. "Iron" is as previously stated. All these are in Capricorn. "Aquatic products" are whatever is produced in water. "Fruits" are well-known. "Gems" and "diverse forms" refer to various shapes and types of precious stones. These are in Aquarius. And so says **Kashyapa**: "In Capricorn: gold, molasses, and minerals. In Aquarius: flowers, diverse gems, and things produced in water."

# Now he speaks of Pisces:

In Pisces: pearls, shells, diamonds, cloths, various types of oils, and products of fish are explained. "Pearls" and "shells" are gems produced from water. "Diamonds" <note>original: "vajra"</note> are well-known. "Various types" means many kinds. "Oils" refers to sesame oil and the like. "Products of fish" includes pearls extracted from fish and similar items. All these are explained as being in Pisces. And so says **Kashyapa**: "The Lord <note>the author/sage</note> has declared lotuses, pearls, and the like to be in Pisces."

# Now he speaks of the knowledge of good and bad results for these commodities:

Jupiter situated in the fourth, tenth, second <note>the house of wealth</note>, eleventh <note>the house of gain</note>, seventh, ninth, or fifth from the sign <note>the sign associated with the commodity</note> is a "provider of sight" <note><term>drishtikara</term>: a technical term here meaning a planet that influences the price or quality of the goods</note>. Mercury is a provider of sight in the second, eleventh, tenth, fifth, and eighth houses. Venus <note>original: "Bhrigusuta," son of Bhrigu</note> situated in the sixth or seventh house causes loss; in the remaining houses, the cruel planets <note>malefics like Mars or Saturn</note> situated in the "upachaya" <note><term>upachaya</term>: houses of growth—3, 6, 10, and 11</note> positions give good results, while in other positions they cause loss. "From the sign" means from whichever sign [the commodity belongs to]. If Jupiter is in the 4th, 10th, 2nd, 11th, 7th, 9th, or 5th, he influences those goods. If Mercury <note>the son of the Moon</note> is in the 2nd, 11th, 10th, 5th, or 8th, he is the influencer. Venus in the 6th or 7th causes loss or decay...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of planetary transits (Gochara) and their specific effects on the market value and availability of various commodities. It concludes the 39th chapter of Bhattotpala’s commentary.</meta>

<warning>The manuscript page has significant water staining and discoloration, particularly on the left side, though the text remains mostly legible.</warning>

...situated in the first, second, third, fourth, fifth, eighth, ninth, tenth, eleventh, or twelfth houses, they cast their gaze <note>aspect</note>. The cruel planets—the Sun, Mars, and Saturn—are givers of auspicious results and favorable aspects when positioned in the "Upachaya" houses <note>houses of increase: the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th</note>. In the remaining positions—the first, second, fourth, fifth, seventh, eighth, ninth, and twelfth—they cause loss.

As stated by **Kashyapa**:
"Jupiter, when moving through the fourth, seventh, second, fifth, ninth, or tenth positions, is remembered as the one who casts a favorable eye upon the commodities assigned to those signs. Venus, situated in the sixth or seventh, causes loss; however, when rising in the eleventh or tenth, it brings acquisition of wealth. Mercury is auspicious when so placed. Malefic planets cast a [favorable] gaze only when in the houses of increase, and not otherwise."

Regarding this, a specific rule is given:
For whichever zodiac sign the cruel or malefic planets—the Sun, Mars, and Saturn—are situated in the houses of affliction <note>unfavorable transits</note> but are also in "Upachaya" houses while being strong <note>possessing high positional strength or 'bala'</note>, located in a friendly sign, their own sign, or their own ninth-division <note><term>Navamsha</term>: a 1/9th subdivision of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuning predictions</note>, they actually produce good results. For the commodities associated with that sign, there will be **great dearness** <note>high prices</note>, high value, and they will become difficult to obtain.

As stated by **Kashyapa**:
"When malefic planets are strong and situated in the unfavorable houses of a sign, the commodities of that sign surely become as rare as sugar." <note>original: "shakara-durlabhante," implying a state of extreme scarcity</note>

He says something further:
When the gentle planets <note>benefics</note> are strong in favorable positions for certain signs, there is a "gaze" upon those commodities, resulting in affordability and popularity. For those signs where the gentle/benefic planets—Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus—are in favorable houses (such as Jupiter in the fourth and others mentioned, Mercury in its starting positions, and Venus in the sixth or seventh), and they are strong, the commodities of those signs become affordable <note>fair-priced</note> and well-loved.

As stated by **Kashyapa**:
"When the gentle planets are strong and situated in favorable houses for any signs, they are remembered as the givers of prosperity for those commodities."

He says another thing as well:
Even during a period of "transit affliction" <note><term>Gochara-pida</term>: suffering caused by the current movement of planets</note>, if a sign is aspected by strong benefic planets, it does not cause suffering. The opposite is true for cruel planets. When Jupiter and others are in positions other than the fourth and so on, there is transit affliction; however, if that sign is then aspected or seen by all the strong benefics—Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus—then the affliction does not manifest as strongly. This means the commodities will be neither too expensive nor too cheap. But if the sign is aspected by strong cruel/malefic planets—the Sun, Mars, and Saturn—during a transit affliction, the result is reversed: the commodities mentioned will become very expensive and difficult to find.

->Thus ends the Thirty-Ninth Chapter, named "The Determination of Commodities," in the **Exposition of the Samhita** composed by **Bhattotpala**.<-

# Now the Chapter on the Rise and Fall of Prices is Explained

## First, the purpose is stated for the sake of demonstration:

Excessive rain, meteors, celestial staffs <note>column-like atmospheric phenomena</note>, halos, and mock suns—having observed these portents on the New Moon and the Full Moon, one should predict the specific price fluctuations month by month as the Sun moves through the signs in order. Portents occurring on other lunar days lead to riots, deaths, and diseases.

<margin>Extensive</margin> Excessive rain refers to a surplus of rainfall. A meteor is a falling star. A "staff" is a streak of light in the sky. Having observed these, along with halos and mock suns <note>original: "pratisurya"</note>, as well as dust storms, frost, and "cities of the Gandharvas" <note>fata morgana or complex mirages in the clouds</note>—one must take note of all these portents on the New Moon <note>Amavasya</note> and Full Moon <note>Paurnamasya</note>. One should then declare the specific price variations as the Sun <note>Aditya</note> moves through the signs, starting with Aries <note>Mesha</note>, month by month in sequence. If these portents like excessive rain occur on any other lunar days (excluding the New and Full Moons), they bring trouble to kings and "damara" <note>civil unrest or riots</note>. Through such conflict, there is suffering.

As **Kashyapa** <note>An ancient sage and authority on Vedic sciences</note> says: "Meteors, excessive rain, halos, and mock suns—having observed these portents on the designated lunar days, one should predict the good and bad for the world. The expert in these rules should consider the abundance or famine and the remaining effects month by month; he should not predict based on other days. Portents occurring at other times only grant defects and misfortune to the king."

Now, he explains what one should do when the Sun is in Aries or Taurus during the New or Full Moon:

When the Sun has entered Aries, if one collects and stores grain, or in Taurus, if one stores forest produce and fruits, there will be a profit in the fourth month from then. When the Sun is in Aries and portents are seen on the New or Full Moon, whoever collects grain and stores it, or does the same in Taurus, will receive a profit by selling them in the fourth month.

Now he speaks regarding Gemini:

When the Sun is in Gemini, having gathered all liquid essences <note>original: "rasa" - refers to oils, juices, or syrups</note>, grains, and rice, and having made a collection of them, one obtains a vast profit by selling them in the sixth month.

<margin>Gathering and collecting</margin> Having seen the portents when the Sun is in Gemini, one should gather all "rasas" such as mung beans and the like, grains, and varieties of rice. By making a store of these and selling them in the sixth month, one obtains an extensive profit.

Now he speaks regarding Cancer:

When the Sun is in Cancer, by storing honey, oils, clarified butter <note>ghee</note>, and molasses, one gains double the profit in the second month; there is no loss in price.

When the Sun is in Cancer, one should store honey, fragrances <note>perfumed substances</note>, oils, ghee, and "phanita" <note>thickened sugarcane juice or liquid jaggery</note>...

...a person obtains. When the Sun <note>original: "Savitri"</note>, the solar deity, reaches its zenith, the specific result is the attainment of desired profits. By collecting various types of vessels, jewels, and gems and storing them for half a year—six months—one obtains the desired profit, growth, or even difficulty as requested or predicted. Here, a further specific rule is stated: In whichever zodiac sign the "cool-rayed one" <note>the Moon</note> or the "thousand-rayed one" <note>the Sun</note> is located, if it is joined with or aspected by a "great friend" <note>a planet in a highly favorable temporal relationship</note>, then profit is seen there. In whichever sign, starting from Aries, the Moon or the Sun is seen by a "great friend" or joined with a friend—based on the temporal positions in the 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 10th, 11th, or 12th houses from each other—that sign is where profit is envisioned. This does not occur elsewhere due to the impossibility of such a combination. Since this consideration arises from observing portents during the New Moon and Full Moon, the "cool-rayed" Moon and "thousand-rayed" Sun are made the primary factors. As the sage **Kashyapa** says:

> Whichever sign the Sun or Moon occupies, if it is joined with or aspected by a "great friend," it is remembered as a bringer of profit. If joined with or aspected by malefic planets, the Sun destroys [the profit]. One should not announce results without understanding the state of the planetary houses, or the prediction will be false.

The "cool-rayed" Moon, when joined with the Sun—meaning they are in the same place during a New Moon—and is joined or aspected by benefics like Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus, causes an increase in prices <note>original: "argha," meaning value or market price</note>. If joined or aspected by malefics, it destroys [the value]. Alternatively, during a Full Moon, if the Moon is joined or aspected by benefics, it is remembered as causing an immediate increase in prices on that very day. Just as with benefics, if the Moon is joined or aspected by the two malefic planets, Mars and Saturn, it destroys or ruins the value; it causes a "loss of price." Similarly, in whichever sign the Sun is joined or aspected by benefics, it causes an increase in price; if joined or aspected by malefics, it destroys it. Thus, having observed and understood the states of the planets in each sign, one should declare the good or bad results. As **Kashyapa** says:

> When the Sun and Moon are joined or aspected by gentle planets <note>benefics</note> or occupy the houses of benefics, they cause an increase in price. If situated in the opposite manner, joined with or seen by malefics, they are said to cause a decrease in price. If situated with friends, they are remembered as having a middling effect.

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the ritual of lustration (Nirajanavidhi) from the previous section, focusing on the king's procession and the auspicious signs of his military force.</meta>

<page-num>42</page-num>

Therefore, it is imagined: with rows of swans flying here and there in all directions, following the scent of the wind, he is like the King of Mountains <note>Himalaya</note>. Or rather, bearing the splendor of Indra’s bow <note>a rainbow</note>, wherever he might go, he is adorned with a crown, earrings, and armlets. "Crown" refers to the head-ornament; "earrings" are ear-ornaments; "armlets" are bracelets; he is adorned and decorated by these. Of what kind? Adorned with jewels and diamonds of many colors. "Many colors" means various hues; "jewels" are gems; "diamonds" are *vajra*; he is decorated by these crowns, earrings, and armlets. Likewise, he is colored and radiated by the rays—the light—of many gems. Then, he bears a resemblance to Indra’s bow, because the beauty of Indra’s bow consists of many colors. 

Or rather, like Indra, he should move about surrounded by his retinue. With horses—steeds—as if leaping into the sky, since Indra travels in the sky. Likewise with elephants—great tuskers—as if supporting the earth, since rutting elephants seem to tear the ground. Surrounded by men—human beings—who have conquered their enemies, like the lords of the gods <note>the Devas</note>, because of his extremely haughty appearance. Therefore, it is imagined: like a conqueror.

### And likewise:
-> Adorned with diamonds and pearls, or wearing white garlands, perfumes, and garments; holding a parasol and mounted on the back of an elephant, he appears like the son of Bhrigu <note>the planet Venus</note> beneath the moon and above a cloud. <-

And thus he should go: "Adorned with diamonds and pearls" means he possesses ornaments made of diamonds—hard gems—along with pearls. Likewise, "wearing white garlands, perfumes, and garments" means he has white garlands (flower wreaths), white *chokshas* <note>a specific type of pure substance or paste</note>, white ointments for smearing the body, and white clothes or raiment. "Holding a parasol" means one who has a sunshade held over him. "Mounted on the back of an elephant" means seated upon the back of a tusker. Therefore, it is imagined: the son of Bhrigu—Venus—situated above a cloud and below the moon. The elephant takes the place of the cloud, the parasol takes the place of the moon, and the King takes the place of Venus.

### Now, he describes the fruit of the army:
-> That king whose army has joyful men, horses, and elephants; which shines with the light of stainless weapons; which is free from portents and yet terrifying to the "lotus" of his enemies—he shall soon conquer the earth. <-

The king who possesses such an army quickly conquers—takes possession of—the earth. Of what kind? "With joyful men, horses, and elephants" means where the men, horses, and elephants are exceedingly delighted and happy. "Shining with the light of stainless weapons" means glowing with the mass of rays from swords and other arms. "Free from portents" means without disturbances or ill omens. "Terrifying to the lotus of his enemies" means bringing fear to the assembly of his foes.

**Thus ends the forty-second chapter, named "The Ritual of Lustration," in the *Exposition of the Samhita* composed by Bhattotpala.**

<meta>This page continues the discussion of omens and signs, transitioning here into the specific study of ornithomancy (divination by birds), specifically focusing on the wagtail.</meta>

I shall now proclaim those fruits <note>results or omens</note> which have been spoken of by the sages regarding Dharma. Now, concerning the bird named the Wagtail <term>Khanjanaka: the wagtail bird, considered a significant omen-bearer in Sanskrit literature</term>: I shall describe the results spoken of by sages such as Garga and others regarding the first sighting of this bird on whichever day it is first seen.

The wagtail is observed during the four months starting from the month of Shravana <note>July-August, the start of the rainy season</note>; therefore, its movement and behavior are noted. Regarding its first sighting, there are four types of wagtails. He <note>the author or sage</note> describes their names and their effects:

The one with a thick, high neck and a black throat is called the **Auspicious** <term>Bhadra: meaning blessed or lucky</term>, and it brings good fortune. Those with dark throats and faces are called the **Full** <term>Sampurna: meaning complete or overflowing</term>; they fulfill all desires. One with a spot like a fish on a black throat and white cheeks is the **Empty** <term>Sarikta: meaning void or depleted</term>. One that is yellow is called the **Cow-Yellow** <term>Gopita</term>.

The wagtail that is thick-bodied, with a very high neck and a black throat, is named the **Auspicious** bird; when seen, it produces auspiciousness and welfare. The one that is dark from the face down to the throat is named the **Full**; it fulfills and completes all hopes. Regarding the one with a fish-spot on a black throat and white cheeks: it is the **Empty**, which brings results that are void or empty. The one that is yellow in color is named the **Cow-Yellow** wagtail; when seen, it causes distress and trouble.

As Sage Kashyapa says:
> The one with a thick, high neck and black throat is remembered as the **Auspicious**.
> The one with a black face down to the throat is remembered as the **Full**.
> The one with white cheeks and a black spot on the throat is designated as the **Empty**.
> The one that is yellow is remembered as the **Cow-Yellow**.
One should indicate the various fruits of these birds when they are seen.

Now, he describes what kind of result is produced based on the location where the bird is seen:
If seen on trees with sweet, fragrant fruits and flowers; near reservoirs of water; in holy places; on elephants, horses, snakes, or at the top of palaces, vehicles, and mansions; or if seen near a gathering of people, a sacrifice, a festival, or near kings and Brahmins; or near a stable for horses, or on flags and ceremonial fans; or near gold, white garments, lotuses, or water-lilies; or near a sacrificial offering, a vessel of curd, or a heap of grain—in these places, the wagtail brings **Prosperity** <term>Shri: wealth, beauty, and divine grace</term>.

<page-num>147</page-num>

In these locations, the wagtail seen produces Lakshmi <note>the goddess of fortune</note>. To explain further: on trees that are sweet and fragrant with fruits and flowers; in holy and purifying reservoirs of water; on the heads of elephants, horses, or snakes; in the gardens of temples; in cow-pens; where there is a gathering of virtuous people; at a sacrifice; at a festival such as a wedding; or near a king...

<summary>This page details the divination of the wagtail bird (Khanjanaka), classifying four types based on physical markings and listing the auspicious locations where its appearance signals prosperity.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of ornithomancy (divination by birds), specifically focusing on the wagtail (Khañjana) as an omen for kings and commoners. It follows previous sections detailing the bird's appearance and initial movements.</meta>

The King; the twice-born <note>original: "dvija," referring to Brahmins</note>; in their presence or vicinity; in the elephant stables; in the horse stables; the parasol <note>a symbol of royalty</note>; the banner <note>a specific type of colorful flag</note>; the fly-whisk <note>made of yak-tail hair</note>; and by the word "etcetera," objects such as incense burners and fans are included. Near these, or near the ancestral sacrifice, or near a full treasure-trove: white garments, the lotus, or the blue water-lily <margin>three</margin> <note>likely referring to three types of flowers or a specific classification</note>. In these worshipped or honored places, or in places touched by sacrificial offerings, or where a vessel full of curds is kept, or in the direction of Kubera <note>the North</note>—in these locations [the bird is auspicious].

As it is said:
->If it is in the mud, there is a gain of fine food; if on cow dung, there is wealth of cattle; if on a grassy spot, one obtains clothing; if on a cart, there is the ruin of the country. If on a hide or a heap of bones, there is loss of wealth or imprisonment; on the back of goats or sheep, there is a quick meeting with a beloved.<-

"In the mud" means in mire; if the wagtail is there, it brings the gain of delicious food. Likewise, "on cow dung" means staying on a heap of cow dung, which results in an abundance of cattle. "On a grassy spot" means staying on green grass, which results in the gain of garments or clothes. From being on a cart, there is the "ruin of the country," meaning destruction. On a "hide" <note>original: "ruru-patale," likely a deer skin</note>, there is the loss of "wealth," meaning money. On "leather," there is "imprisonment," meaning being bound. On a place cleared of horses <note>or a stable</note>, there is disease. "Goat" and "sheep" are well known; if it stays on the "back" or top of goats and sheep, it "quickly," meaning very soon, "brings about a meeting with a beloved," meaning a union with a loved one.

As it is said:
->On buffaloes, camels, donkeys, bones, cemeteries, corners, gravel, ramparts, or heaps of ash, it quickly brings disease, sorrow, and fear.<-

In these places, the wagtail seen is always inauspicious; it brings disease and does not provide protection. It brings "sorrow and fear"; seeing it on these parts gives "fear to men." In which places? On buffaloes, camels, young camels, donkeys, bones, in the "cemetery" <note>original: "pitṛ-vane," forest of the ancestors</note>, in corners, on gravel, on "clods of earth," on "watchtowers," on "rampart walls," or in "heaps of ash"—in these places [it is bad].

Now he describes the characteristics of its movement:
->Shaking the wings is inauspicious; drinking water in a river is auspicious; at sunrise it is praised, but the wagtail at sunset does not give desired results.<-

"Shaking the wings" means vibrating the feathers; this is inauspicious and gives undesired results. "River" means a stream; "drinking water" there is an auspicious sight. Some say drinking water is auspicious when seen in a place where water flows. At "sunrise," meaning the time of the sun's rising, the wagtail is "praised," meaning it gives auspicious results. At "sunset," it gives "undesired results," meaning inauspiciousness.

As it is said:
->When the Lustration of Arms ceremony is completed, in whichever direction the King sees the wagtail going, the enemy coming from that direction quickly falls under his power.<-

"Completed" means finished. After the "Lustration of Arms" <note>original: "Nīrājanā," a royal ritual involving the purification of weapons and horses</note>, in whichever "direction" or quarter the King "sees" or observes the wagtail "going" or traveling, the "enemy" or foe of the King "coming from that direction" "quickly," meaning very soon, "falls under his power," meaning he becomes submissive or subject to his will.

<summary>This page details the omens associated with the wagtail bird's location and behavior, explaining how its presence on royal symbols or clean ground signifies prosperity, while its presence on bones or ash foretells ruin. It also notes the bird's significance during the royal Nīrājanā ceremony for predicting military success.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discourse on the wagtail bird (Khanjana) as an omen, specifically focusing on how its behaviors indicate buried treasure and how to mitigate bad omens through ritual.</meta>

# Now, he speaks to demonstrate the signs of proof:
Where the bird engages in mating, there a treasure <term>Nidhi: a hidden store of wealth or a foundation</term> exists. Where it vomits <note>original: "svarkardayati"</note>, there will be a streak of mica <note>original: "lestika/kacha"; likely referring to mineral deposits</note>. Where it leaves its droppings, the sages—such as Kashyapa and others—teach that charcoal will be found. One should dig the earth to remove any doubt.

In the place where it "goes to mate," in that specific spot of ground, there is a treasure. Where it vomits, there a "streak" or "glassy vein" exists. Where this wagtail <term>Khanjana: the Pied Wagtail, considered a highly significant bird in Indian augury</term> leaves its droppings and does not move far from them, the sages like Kashyapa say there is charcoal beneath the soil.

As Kashyapa says: "Where it performs mating, there one should point out a treasure. Where it vomits sour substance, there mica or glass may be found. Where it leaves droppings, there one should indicate charcoal. To remove curiosity <note>or doubt</note>, one should dig the earth." To resolve the doubt arising from the sight of the "deer-eyed" bird, one should dig the ground so that proof may arise <unclear>clearly</unclear>.

## Now, he speaks of the good and bad omens:
A bird that is dead, mutilated, wounded, or diseased gives a result equal to its own state. One that is perched on a fence or wall <note>original: "villyamanako"</note> bestows wealth, but also brings about imprisonment or a meeting with relatives.

Wagtails that are dead, mutilated, wounded, or diseased give a result equal to their own bodies. For example: if a dead one is seen, it gives death to the seer. Likewise, a mutilated one gives mutilation, a wounded one gives injury to the body, and a diseased one gives only disease.

## Now, he speaks of the ritual when a favorable wagtail is seen:
Even a king, having seen a favorable bird in a favorable place, should offer an <term>Arghya: a respectful offering of water, flowers, or grain</term> upon the surface of the earth, using fragrant flowers. He should welcome the good omen to ensure its increase.

A king, having seen a bird with excellent characteristics in a favorable location, should perform an offering on the ground with fragrant flowers and incense. In this way, he welcomes the good result and causes it to increase.

<margin>158</margin>

## Now, he speaks of the ritual when an unfavorable wagtail is seen:
Even after seeing an unfavorable wagtail, a king will not encounter misfortune if he remains devoted to the worship of the twice-born <note>Brahmins</note>, teachers, holy men, and gods, and if he abstains from eating meat for seven days.

If a king sees a wagtail that brings undesirable results, but remains intent on the worship of Brahmins, instructors, virtuous men, and the deities, then he does not obtain the bad result—provided that for seven days he does not consume meat.

## Now, he speaks to show the timing of the results:
From the first sighting, the result lasts for a year. For daily sightings, the result lasts for the remainder of that day. The direction, the location, and the physical signs...

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of *Prashna Shastra* (horary astrology/divination), specifically focusing on *Nimitta* (omens) derived from the querent's physical gestures and the objects they happen to be holding or looking at during the consultation.</meta>

If the querent touches their qualities <note>likely referring to the limbs or specific body parts</note>, it signifies grief regarding a daughter; if they touch their head, it indicates fear from the government or a king. **Another interpretation:** If they touch their chest, it foretells separation; if they cover their body with a cloth, it brings misfortune. If they place one foot upon the other while touching a cloth behind them, it indicates the arrival of something dear.

If the querent sighs while touching their chest, one should predict separation. If they cover their own body with a cloth <term>Karpatavriti: the act of wrapping oneself in a garment</term>, it will be "Anarthada," meaning it will bring undesirable results. If the querent places one foot upon the second foot while touching a cloth, there will be "Priyaptih," the gain of something beloved.

->**As Parashara says:**<-
*Touching the chest indicates separation; casting off a garment brings the arrival of misfortune. If one foot touches the other, or if one touches a cloth behind them, the wise man should know that a meeting with a loved one is coming.*

**Another interpretation:** If the querent scratches the earth with their big toe, they are troubled by a great anxiety. If they scratch their feet with their hands, they are thinking about a female slave or servant.

If the querent, out of great anxiety, scratches the earth (the ground) with their big toe, or scratches their feet with their hands, then that thought is "Dasi-maya," concerning a female servant.

->**As Parashara says:**<-
*Scratching the earth with the big toe indicates a great anxiety; scratching the feet with the hand indicates a thought regarding a female servant.*

**Another interpretation:** Upon seeing a palm leaf or a rhythmic tapping, one should think of clothing. If one sees hair, grass, bone, ash, or soil, a disease is taking hold. If one sees a rope, a net, or bark, it signifies imprisonment.

If a palm leaf or a rhythmic gesture is seen, the querent is thinking of "Anshuka" (clothing). If hair, grass, rice-husks, bone, ash, or soil are encountered—hair is *Kacha*, grass is *Trina*, bone and ash are well known—if any of these are present, "Vyadhi" (disease or pain) takes hold of the querent. If a rope, a net, or "Valkala" (tree bark) are seen or held by the querent, then one should predict "Bandhanam" (imprisonment or bondage).

->**As Parashara says:**<-
*Upon seeing a palm leaf, the thought is of cloth. Upon seeing hair, bone, or ash, one should speak of the fear of disease. Upon seeing chains, ropes, or bark, there is the fear of imprisonment.*

**Another interpretation:** By the sight of long pepper, black pepper, *Shringi*, or *Varida* <note>nut-grass</note>; by *Rodhra*, *Kushta*, *Vasuna*, *Ambu*, or cumin; by *Gandhamamsi* or *Shatapushpa* <note>dill</note>, one should tell what is being thought about.

The thoughts regarding women, men, those afflicted by faults, the healthy, sons, wealth, or grain are declared through the sight of these items.

By seeing long pepper (*Pippali*) and the rest, one should tell the thoughts in order. Among them, seeing long pepper indicates a thought regarding a woman who is afflicted by faults or impurity. Seeing black pepper (*Maricha*) indicates a thought regarding a man who is sinful or afflicted by faults. Seeing *Shringi* indicates a thought of one who is afflicted, diseased, or dead. Seeing *Varida* (Musta/nut-grass) indicates a thought of their destruction. Seeing *Rodhra* indicates the destruction of wealth.

<summary>This page details how a diviner interprets the querent's physical movements—such as scratching the ground or touching the chest—and the presence of specific objects like spices or bones to determine the hidden subject of their inquiry. It relies heavily on the authority of the sage Parashara to correlate these omens with outcomes like illness, imprisonment, or the gain of wealth.</summary>

# Page 175

If one sees **teeth**, it signifies the loss of a son. <note>original: "sutanāśakṛtām"</note> Seeing **new clothes** signifies the loss of wealth. Seeing **water** or a **young boy** signifies the loss of grain. Seeing **cumin** <note>original: "jīrakam" or "ajājī"</note> signifies the loss of a son. Seeing **hooves** <note>of animals</note> signifies the destruction of two-legged creatures <note>human servants or laborers</note>. Seeing **dill** <note>original: "śatapuṣpā"</note> signifies the destruction of four-legged animals and two-legged creatures. Seeing a **townsman** <note>original: "nagaraka"</note> signifies anxiety regarding the loss of land. By observing these mentioned objects, the causes of destruction are asked about.

**As Parashara says:**
Seeing **long pepper** <note>original: "pippalī"</note> indicates anxiety caused by a wicked woman. Seeing **yellow sandalwood** <note>original: "paricampa"</note> indicates anxiety caused by an evil man. Seeing **hero's-flesh** <note>likely a specific plant or meat</note> indicates anxiety over death. Seeing **cumin** indicates the loss of a son. Seeing **lodh tree bark** <note>original: "rodhra"</note> indicates the loss of grain. Seeing **pearls** <note>original: "mukta"</note> indicates loss. Seeing **costus** <note>original: "kuṣṭha"</note> indicates the loss of a son. Seeing **clothing** indicates the loss of wealth. Seeing **lead** <note>original: "dvīvīra"</note> indicates the loss of grain. Seeing a **city** indicates the loss of land. Seeing **dill** indicates the destruction of crossroads. Seeing **flesh** indicates the destruction of two-legged creatures.

### On Auspicious Signs (Dashas)
The presence of **Trayodha** <note>likely a specific fruit or ritual item</note>, **Mahua** <note>original: "madhūka"</note>, **Tinduka**, **Fig** <note>original: "jantu"</note>, **Mango**, **Jujube** <note>original: "badara"</note>, and **Nutmeg** <note>original: "jātiphala"</note> results in the acquisition of wealth, gold, men, iron, clothes, and elephants.

If these fruits are held in the hand when a question is asked, wealth is obtained. Specifically:
*   **Trayodha fruit:** Gain of wealth.
*   **Mahua fruit:** Gain of gold.
*   **Tinduka fruit:** Gain of a "two-legged" being (a man or servant).
*   **Fig fruit:** Gain of iron.
*   **Mango fruit:** Gain of cloth or garments.
*   **Jujube fruit:** Gain of a cluster of figs <note>or wealth associated with the Udumbara tree</note>.

**As Parashara says:**
When a question is asked while holding **Trayodha** fruits in the hand, one should predict the arrival of wealth. **Mahua** and **Udumbara** fruits signify the arrival of gold. **Tinduka** signifies the arrival of a person. **Mango** signifies the arrival of clothes. **Jujube** signifies silver or copper. **Fig** fruits signify iron.

### On Further Signs
A vessel filled with **grain**, a **full pot** <note>original: "kumbha"</note>, and the **Udumbara tree** are signs of increase. However, the **excrement** of elephants, cows, and dogs signifies the destruction of wealth and prosperity respectively.

A vessel overflowing with grain or a full water pot indicates prosperity. Seeing the dung of an **elephant** signifies the destruction of wealth and power. Seeing **cow dung** signifies the "destruction" <note>or downfall</note> of young women. Seeing **dog dung** signifies the destruction of enemies <note>original: "turuda", possibly referring to a specific class of foe</note>.

**As Parashara says:**
Upon seeing a grain vessel or a full pot, one should know it as the "increase of the Udumbara" <note>general prosperity</note>. Seeing elephant dung signifies the loss of power or wealth. Cow dung signifies the misconduct of women. Dog dung signifies the destruction of enemies.

### On Auspicious Animals
Seeing **cattle, elephants, buffaloes, lotuses, silver,** or **moist items** while they are in a happy state indicates the arrival of wealth, sandalwood, silk, and a collection of ornaments.

If these things are seen behind one's back or observed without being sought, one shall obtain a collection of new ornaments and life-breath <note>meaning vitality or success</note>. In that regard, upon seeing a **lotus**, one obtains blankets or woolen clothes from an Avruka or Aushika <note>likely specific regions or tribes known for textiles</note>. Upon seeing an **elephant** or a herd of elephants, there is an arrival of wealth. Upon seeing a **buffalo**, one who is naked obtains silken garments <note>original: "kaumavastra"</note>. Upon seeing a **lotus flower**, or **sandalwood** from the Malaya mountains, or **silver** <note>original: "rupya"</note>, or a **horse**, there is the arrival of ornaments.

**And so says Parashara:**
> From a buffalo, the arrival of silken garments and gems; from cows and deer-skins, the arrival of superior ornaments; from a lotus, the arrival of red garments and sandalwood; from silver, the arrival of silken clothes.

He says further:
If one sees an **old Shravaka** <note>a Jain or Buddhist lay follower</note> or a **Kapalika** <note>a member of a specific Shaivite ascetic sect</note>, then for the men observing them, there is a thought regarding gambling or a loss incurred for the sake of a friend. Upon seeing a **wandering monk** <note>original: "parivrajaka"</note>, the thought in the seeker's heart concerns a courtesan or a woman of high status <note>original: "ganika"</note>.

He says further:
The **Shakya** <note>Buddhist monk</note>, the **Teacher** <note>Upadhyaya</note>, the **Arhat** <note>Jain monk</note>, the **Nirgrantha** <note>naked ascetic</note>, the **Soothsayer** <note>Nimittika</note>, the **Merchant-guild member**, the **Fisherman**, the **Thief**, the **Army Commander**, and the **Merchant**; [these indicate] a female slave, a warrior, or those to be executed.

Upon seeing a **Shakya**, the thought is of thieves; upon seeing a **Teacher**, it concerns an army commander; upon seeing an **Arhat**, it concerns a merchant; upon seeing a **Nirgrantha**, it concerns a female slave; upon seeing a **Soothsayer** <note>one who knows the divine signs</note>, it concerns a warrior; upon seeing a **Guild-member**, it concerns a prominent merchant of the market; upon seeing a **Fisherman**, the thought is of execution or killing.

He says further:
When an **Ascetic** or a **Vintner** <note>liquor-seller</note> is seen, it concerns one who has gone abroad or the tending of cattle. If seen behind or if the two are seen in a state of distress, it indicates failure.

When an **Ascetic** is observed, the thought in the heart of the seeker concerns someone who is away on a journey. When a **Vintner** is seen, the thought is of cattle-tending. If these two are seen intoxicated or distressed, the thought is of a failed purpose.

**And so says Parashara:**
> Upon seeing a Nirgrantha, a female slave is in the heart; upon seeing an old Shravaka, a friend or a loss; for a Shakya, it is regarding thieves; for a wandering monk, it is a woman of high status or a courtesan; for a Teacher, an army commander; for a Guild-member, a prominent merchant; for a Soothsayer, a warrior; for a Buddhist, a merchant; for those with shaven heads, a failed purpose; for an Ascetic, one who is abroad; for a Vintner, the tending of cattle; for a Fisherman, killing and slaughter.

Furthermore, he says:
"I desire this," "A good path," "May the noble ones command you"—these indicate union, enjoyment, and the arrival of lordship. When "I desire this" or "Good" is spoken, one should declare the thought to be about union or power...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of divination (Prasna Shastra), specifically focusing on identifying the nature of a loss and the identity of a thief based on the querent's speech and physical gestures.</meta>

<page-num>176</page-num>

If the querent speaks of "obtaining a son" or "profit," one should declare that the thought is about wealth. If they speak of "what was taken by sons yesterday," it concerns a worry. **Now regarding the ten stages:** If the words "Declare," "Victory," or "Banner" are spoken, or if they say "Look into my house and tell me what I am thinking," or "Speak in the midst of all people," or "Tell me the truth," then the theft was committed by a kinsman or a thief known to the family. When the words "Declare" or "Speak" are uttered, or words like "Victory" or "Banner" arise, or "Look back at my house and tell me what was taken," it indicates a theft by a relative. If they say "Speak in the midst of all people," it implies the thief is among their own power or intellect <note>The text suggests the thief is someone within the querent's immediate social circle</note>, and thus the worry regarding the stolen item is known to be caused by a thief from among the kinsmen.

> And so says Parashara <note>An ancient sage and legendary authority on astrology and law</note>: If the sun is mentioned, it is a worry about the body; if "wealth" is mentioned, it is a worry about the family; if "taken" is mentioned, it is about a union; if "afterward" or "for one's own sake" is mentioned, it is about profit; if "declare" is mentioned, it is about a journey. If they say "Victory," "Aṣṭakā," or "O Teacher, tell me," or "Look back," it concerns a kinsman. If they sigh deeply or suddenly call out in the midst of their own people, one should know it is a worry regarding a thief.

## ## Regarding the Science of Identifying a Thief

If an internal limb is touched, the thief is a kinsman; if an external limb is touched, the thief is an outsider. By the touching of the big toe or the toes, it is a male or female servant. By the shanks, it is a messenger; by the navel, a sister; by the thighs or heart, it is the wife; and by the touching of the fingers of the hand, it is a son or daughter. If an internal part of the body is touched when the question is asked, the thief is said to be a "kinsman" (one's own). If an external limb is touched, the thief is said to be an "outsider." If the big toe is touched, the thief is a male servant; if the other toes are touched, it is a female servant. Touching the shanks indicates a "messenger" or laborer. The navel indicates a sister. The heart or thighs indicate one's own wife. Touching the fingers of the hand indicates a son; touching a cluster of fingers indicates one's own daughter is the thief. Thus, by the act of touching, the knowledge of the thief is obtained.

### ### For Example:
If the belly is touched, it is the mother; the head, the teacher; the right and left arms, the brother and the brother's wife respectively. One should thus point out the thief.
If the "jagara" (stomach/belly) is touched, the mother is the thief. If the head is touched, the teacher. If the right or left arms are touched, then respectively: the right arm indicates the brother, and the left indicates his wife. In this manner, by the touching of limbs, one should declare the "taskara" <term>Taskara: A Sanskrit term for a thief or robber</term>.

> And so says Parashara: By touching an external limb, an external thief; an internal limb, one's own. Big toe: male servant. Toes: female servant. Shanks: messenger. Belly: mother. Fingers of the hand: son or daughter. Thumbs: son. Navel: sister. Thighs, head, or heart: the wife. Right arm: brother. Left arm: brother's wife.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of divination through omens (Nimitta), specifically focusing on how the physical actions and surroundings of a person asking a question (the querent) predict whether lost property will be recovered or if a sick person will survive.</meta>

### On the Recovery of Lost Property and Omens of Death

If the querent, while asking a question, touches their outer limbs instead of their inner body parts, or if they discharge **phlegm** <note>Sanskrit: shleshma</note>, **urine**, or **feces** at that moment, the lost item will not be recovered. The same result is predicted if they drop a cloth held in their palm, or if they excessively crack their joints <note>literally: "making a chata-chata sound"</note> by bending their limbs. Furthermore, if they happen to see an empty vessel <note>an omen of lack or depletion</note> or a thief while asking, or if they hear unpleasant sounds such as falling, giving away, forgetting, destruction, confusion, departing, robbery, or death, the stolen or lost object is not regained.

As the sage **Parashara** <note>A revered ancient authority on astrology and law</note> says:
> "If one avoids the inner limbs and touches the outer, or discharges waste, or drops something, or cracks the body joints; or if there is the sound of falling, robbing, forgetting, or destruction; or if one sees empty vessels or thieves—know that the lost item is gone for good."

### Signs of Death for the Afflicted
Now, the knowledge of death for those suffering from illness is explained. All the signs previously mentioned—along with the sight of **chaff**, **bones**, or **poison**—bring about death for the sick. If the querent hears the sound of weeping or wailing while asking about a patient, it indicates the end.

**Parashara** further states:
> "For those afflicted by disease, the sight of impure things like hair, bones, ashes, chaff, or poison, or the hearing of words such as 'cut,' 'broken,' 'perished,' 'killed,' 'struck,' 'bound,' or 'torn,' or the sound of weeping, indicates the death of the patient."

### Knowledge Regarding Food
Next, the knowledge of food is discussed. If the querent touches a limb and speaks while standing or stumbling, it indicates the nature of the meal consumed. If they touch an internal part and speak much, it suggests they have eaten a very large amount of food...

# Page 177

The messenger, standing as described, should speak as follows. Now, if the following occurs: from the touching of the **forehead** or the sight of the **face**, one should say **Shali rice** <note>A high-quality variety of translucent rice</note> was eaten. From the sound "Uu" or the touching of the **neck and mouth**, one should say **Shashtika rice** <note>Rice that ripens in sixty days</note> or **Yavaka** <note>Barley-gruel</note> was consumed. If the questioner touches their forehead or if the messenger sees their face, it should be stated that the questioner has eaten Shali rice. From the sound "Uu," a specific type of grain is indicated, and by touching the neck and mouth, barley-meal is known.

Now, if the following occurs: touching the **belly, breasts, or knees** indicates **beans, milk, sesame, or barley**. If the person tastes something or if these are written down, the first among them should be understood as the sign. Touching the belly signifies **Masha beans**; touching the breasts signifies **milk-rice** <note>rice boiled in milk, often sweetened</note>; touching the lower abdomen signifies **sesame-rice**; and touching the knees signifies **barley**. If one sees them tasting something or if it is written, and it is the eighth day of the lunar fortnight, it should be known that they have eaten something of **sweet flavor**.

Now, if the following occurs: if the **tongue** is moved or contracted, it signifies **water**; if the **mouth** is twitching, it signifies **pungent** flavors; if it is dry, then **astringent**; if there is a bitter taste, then **bitter**; and if they lick their lips, then **salt**. When the tongue contracts, it is said they have consumed water. When the mouth twitches, they have eaten something pungent. If the palate is dry, it is astringent; if the taste is bitter, or if they act as if they have tasted salt, they will seek to drink.

Now, if the following occurs: by hearing or seeing a **carnivore**, one should say **meat** was eaten. By touching the **arm**, it is **venison**; by other signs, it is **fowl**. This is the cause. If a carnivore is mentioned, the food is meat; if it is a small carnivore, it is the fourth type of meat-food. By hearing or seeing these, one says meat-mixed food was eaten. By touching the arm, venison; by other signs, bird meat. These are the stated signs or omens.

Now, if the following occurs: touching the **head, throat, hair, or back** indicates **elephant, buffalo, ram, or peacock** meat. By touching the head and other parts, the meat of elephants and other animals should be declared as eaten. Touching the head signifies **elephant meat**; touching the throat signifies **buffalo**; touching the hair signifies **mutton**; touching the <unclear>jaw</unclear> signifies **pork**; touching a corpse signifies elephant meat; touching the back signifies **rabbit**; touching the shanks signifies **deer**; touching the bladder signifies **buffalo** meat.

Now, if the following occurs: if an **ill-omened bird** is seen or heard, one should speak of **monitor lizard or fish** meat. In a question regarding a **pregnant woman**, such a fall of an omen indicates a **miscarriage**. If an unlucky bird is observed or a bad omen is heard, one should say they have eaten monitor lizard or fish. Likewise, in a query about a pregnancy, if a bad omen is heard, the woman's pregnancy will result in a fall <note>meaning a miscarriage or unsuccessful birth</note>.

One should specify the potential for miscarriage <note>original: "garbhaprapatanam"</note> based on these signs. And so says the sage Parashara: Now, by observing the food consumed, one may know the outcome. In this regard, if the forehead is touched, the food eaten was rice with clarified butter; if the nose is touched, it was rice mixed with fragrant herbs or lotus; if the eyes are touched <gloss>specifically</gloss>, it was rice with sesame; if the ears, rice with black gram; if the breasts, rice with milk; if the knees, barley gruel. If the person is seen licking their lips, they have eaten sour gruel; if the mouth is dry, something pungent; if the throat is constricted, something astringent; if the person is sighing, something bitter; if they have little oiliness on the skin, something salty. If one sees a scavenger bird, they have eaten meat. If there is contact with a horn, a tail, or a camel, or the sound of a bird, or if the feet, shanks, or hair are touched, or if there is a falling away of clothing, or if the neck is touched, it indicates buffalo meat. If one sees something torn, cut, or split, it indicates the meat of a monitor lizard or fish.

### On the Knowledge of the Birth of the Pregnant Woman

Now he explains the knowledge of the birth: One may know if it is a male, female, or eunuch by what is seen, inferred, or standing in front, or by what is touched; that birth will occur. Seeing flowers or fruit is auspicious.

When asked "What will be born?" of a woman carrying a fetus, the birth is determined by what is seen, inferred, spoken, thought, or what stands in front or is touched. If a man is seen, inferred, stands in front, or is touched, a male is born. Likewise for a woman, a female birth. If a neutral or third-gender person <term>eunuch: "napumsaka", referring to non-binary or sterile categories in traditional medicine</term> is seen, inferred, stands in front, or is touched, a neutral birth occurs. Regarding the sight of flowers and fruit: if one sees a small amount of food, or flowers (blossoms), or fruits, it is an auspicious birth, meaning an easy delivery.

He also stated another method: By touching the brow or the belly with the thumb or fingers, one should ask about the thought in the heart. This is determined by honey, clarified butter, gold, gems, coral, or by the presence of the mother, a nurse, or a son.

If the woman touches her two eyebrows or her belly with her thumb or fingers and asks, then the thought regarding the fetus should be known by the expert. Or, by those things standing in front: <gloss>honey and grapes</gloss>, or by "middle-clarified butter" and the like <gloss>clarified butter is "ghritam"</gloss>, or by things with masculine names or beautiful fruits. Likewise <gloss>gold is "suvarnam"</gloss>, by gold, gems, and coral <gloss>gems are rubies; coral is "vidrumam"</gloss>. Also by the mother (the biological mother), the nurse (the wet-nurse), or the son (the offspring)—by the presence of these, one should know the state of the fetus.

And so says Parashara: Now, if she touches the brows or the belly with the thumb or fingers and asks, one should know the condition of the fetus. Likewise, by the presence of fruit, wood, rubbed coral, honey, or clarified butter, or by the words "mother," "nurse," or "son," the specific state of the pregnancy is revealed.

He also stated another point: The birth of the one with a fetus, or their illness, is drawn out by the power of the observed omens; if the omen is distressed, the birth is difficult, and if the omen is pleasant, the birth is easy.

# Page 178

<note>The text describes a system of "Anga-sparsha-vidya" or the science of limb-touching, where a physician or diviner predicts the health and gender of a fetus based on which part of the body is touched.</note>

When the belly is touched by the hand, or when the breast is touched, if it is a female fetus <note>original: "stri-garbha"</note>, it is as if she is a daughter. At that same moment, if due to the influence of bad omens <note>original: "dushta-nimitta"</note>, or from seeing a bad omen, or from the fear of a fall or destruction, the fault does not fall upon the men. Alternatively, if there is pain or pressure in the belly, or rubbing and pulling of the womb, whether by the hand or by leaning, it still gives a result.

### Knowledge of the Timing for the Pregnant Woman

Now he explains the knowledge of timing <note>original: "kala-jnanam"</note> for the pregnant woman. If the right nostril is touched by the thumb, one should predict an increase in flesh <note>likely referring to healthy growth</note>. If the left is touched, then two ears, and so on. Regarding the four: if the right ear is touched with the thumb, one should say there will be an illness of the fetus <note>original: "garbha-ruga"</note> after one month. If the left ear is touched, it will be after two years. Thus, by the word "month" <note>original: "masa"</note>, a month is indicated, and by "four," the breasts. If the hole of the right ear is touched, the flesh is doubled, and there is fetal illness in two months; if the left, in two years. By touching the breast, in four months; by touching both breasts, the same.

### Another Method

At the base of the braided hair <note>original: "veni-mule"</note>, if touched, one should say she will give birth to sons. Starting from the little finger up to the thumb in sequence, it indicates five sons. By touching the little finger, one son is born; the ring finger, two; the middle finger, three; the forefinger, four; and the thumb, five. If the big toe of the foot or the pair of heels is touched, she will give birth to one daughter.

### Further Methods

By touching the left or right thigh, she gives birth to one or two. If the middle of the forehead is touched, she will have four daughters; if the end of the forehead is touched, she will have three daughters, and so on.

> Thus says the sage Parashara <note>A legendary Vedic seer and author of early medical and astrological texts</note>: Regarding the touching of the belly, one should speak only to the pregnant woman. If the thumb touches the right nostril, there is fetal illness within a month; if the left, after two years. If the ear-hole is touched, in two months; if the left, in two years. If the breasts are touched with the thumb, in four months. If she scratches her belly due to a pale or weak humor <note>term: dhatu - the fundamental bodily tissues in Ayurveda</note>, or if she sees or hears signs of falling, shifting, or fear, one should know the loss of the limb of the fetus. Likewise, by seeing or hearing the sounds of four-legged animals, fish, etc., if they have masculine names, a son is known; if feminine names, a female birth; if of neuter form, a neuter birth is known from the touching of the limbs.

If the woman touches a cow’s hoof-print, one should know a male birth will occur; if she touches signs designated as feminine, a female birth; and if she touches signs designated as neuter, a neuter birth.

## Now, the Specifics:
If she touches the base of her braided hair <note>original: "venimula"</note> on a flat surface, one should declare that she will give birth to three sons from a single "wheel" <note>likely referring to a whorl of hair or a specific circular omen</note>. Touching the middle of the forehead indicates four children. Touching the forehead in general indicates three. Touching the ears indicates five children. Touching the palm of the hand indicates three. By touching the little finger, ring finger, middle finger, index finger, and thumb, one should know there will be one, two, three, four, and five children respectively. If she touches the right side, she will give birth to two sons in a "right-hand wheel"; on the left side, three daughters and two sons. On the wheel of the big toe, one child on each side, or one wheel on each side.

## Now, for the knowledge of which Nakshatra <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 lunar mansions or star clusters in Indian astrology</term> the creature's birth will occur in:
The head, forehead, ears, cheeks, jaw, teeth, throat, the right and left sides of the neck, the heart, the chin, the hands, the chest, the breasts, the right and left sides of the heart, the belly, the hips, the buttocks, the anus, the joints, the two thighs, the knees, the shanks, and the feet—these correspond to the stars beginning with Krittika.

When the time of delivery is near, if the pregnant woman <term>Garbhini: a pregnant woman</term> touches her body starting from the head, one should recognize the Nakshatras starting from Krittika. Specifically:
*   If she touches her **head**, the birth occurs in the **Krittika** Nakshatra.
*   If she touches her **forehead**, it is **Rohini**.
*   If she touches her **eyebrows**, it is **Mrigashira**.
*   If she touches her **ears**, it is **Ardra**.
*   If she touches her **cheeks**, it is **Punarvasu**.
*   If she touches her **heart**, it is **Pushya**.
*   If she touches her **teeth**, it is **Ashlesha**.
*   If she touches her **throat** (the neck), it is **Magha**.
*   If she touches her **right shoulder**, it is **Purva Phalguni**.
*   If she touches her **left shoulder**, it is **Uttara Phalguni**.
*   If she touches her **hands**, it is **Hasta**.
*   If she touches her **chin**, it is **Chitra**.
*   If she touches the **base of the neck** (below the chin), it is **Swati**.
*   If she touches her **chest**, it is **Vishakha**.
*   If she touches her **right breast**, it is **Anuradha**.
*   If she touches her **left breast**, it is **Jyeshtha**.
*   If she touches the **pit of the heart**, it is **Mula**.
*   If she touches her **right side**, it is **Purva Ashadha**.
*   If she touches her **left side**, it is **Uttara Ashadha**.
*   If she touches her **belly**, it is **Shravana**.
*   If she touches her **hips**, it is **Dhanishta**.
*   If she touches her **knees**, it is **Revati**.
*   If she touches her **shanks**, it is **Ashvini**.
*   If she touches her **feet**, it is **Bharani**.

### Thus says the Sage Parashara:
<note>Parashara is a legendary Vedic sage and the father of Vyasa, considered a foundational authority in Indian astrology (Jyotisha).</note>
Touching the head indicates birth in Krittika; the forehead, Rohini; the eyebrows, Mrigashira; the ears, Ardra; the cheeks, Punarvasu; the heart, Pushya; the teeth, Ashlesha; the neck, Magha; the right shoulder, Purva Phalguni; the left shoulder, Uttara Phalguni; the hands, Hasta; the chin, Chitra; the throat-stalk, Swati; the chest, Vishakha; the right breast, Anuradha; the left breast, Jyeshtha; the pit of the heart, Mula; the right side, Purva Ashadha; the other (left) side, Uttara Ashadha; the belly, Shravana; the hips, Dhanishta; the buttocks, Shatabhisha <note>original: "Varuna," the deity ruling Shatabhisha</note>; the right thigh, Purva Bhadrapada; the left thigh, Uttara Bhadrapada; the knees, Revati; the shanks, Ashvini; and the feet, Bharani.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of Angavidya (the science of bodily signs) from the previous folio, transitioning from the summary of limb-touching to the specific characteristics of skin eruptions or marks.</meta>

### Now, he speaks for the purpose of conclusion:

Thus has been declared this clear system of signs regarding the touching of limbs, for the attainment of desired goals. Having thoroughly examined the scriptures <note>original: "shastras"</note>, a person of vast intellect and noble character who understands this secret of the mantras <note>or "counsel"</note> is honored by kings and the masses alike, O Somadeva! The word "Thus" <note>iti</note> is used here for conclusion or manner. This "touching of the limbs" refers to the characteristics of physical contact, which has been composed very clearly. By doing what? By thoroughly seeing and examining the traditional scriptures composed by sages such as Garga, Parashara, and others. For what purpose? For the attainment of desired results. One who possesses a vast, expansive intellect and is noble—meaning they have fully grasped everything without exception—such an astrologer <note>original: "daivajnah"</note> is always and at all times honored or worshipped by kings and groups of people.

## Thus ends the forty-ninth chapter, named "The Science of Limbs" <term>Angavidya: the divination of bodily movements and marks</term>, in the Commentary on the Samhita.

Beyond this, the characteristics of pustules or marks <term>Pitaka-lakshana: the interpretation of moles, spots, or inflammatory marks on the skin</term> are also discussed elsewhere. That is now explained by us. There, he describes the characteristics of marks according to the four social classes <note>original: "varnas"</note>:

White, red, yellow, and black are the marks for the Brahmins and the others respectively. These marks, when appearing on the specific classes, yield the results previously mentioned. For the four classes starting with the Brahmins, the marks are, in order: white, red, yellow, and black. Specifically: white for Brahmins, red for Kshatriyas <note>the warrior/ruler class</note>, yellow for Vaishyas <note>the merchant/farmer class</note>, and black for Shudras <note>the laborer class</note>. In this sequence, the marks mentioned yield the stated fruits for those specific classes, but not if they appear on those born of other classes. This means: white marks give results for Brahmins, red for Kshatriyas, and they do not overlap; yellow for Vaishyas, and black for Shudras.

### Now, he speaks of the specific results:

Marks on the head that are oily and beautiful bring a heap of wealth. On the forehead, they bring immediate good fortune. Marks arising on the pair of eyebrows bring misfortune. Those arising in the middle—at the junction of the eyebrows—bring the meeting of beloved kin, but also bad character. Marks located on the eyelids bring sorrow. Located in the eyes, they bring the sight of desired things. In the region of the temples, they indicate renunciation <note>original: "pravrajya"</note>. Those located in the path of tears <note>the tear ducts/cheeks</note> bring constant anxiety or grief.

Three marks on the head that are oily, pleasing, and possess luster and beauty bring a collection of wealth. If seen on the head, they quickly bring good fortune. Marks arising on the two eyebrows bring misfortune. Those arising in the middle—born at the union of the brows—bring the meeting of dear relatives and also "bad character" <note>original: "duhshilata"</note> very quickly. Marks on the eyelids produce sorrow. In the eyes, they bring the sight of pleasant things. In the temple region, they indicate the life of a wandering ascetic. Those in the place where tears fall bring constant worry and anxiety. Also—

# On the Marks of the Body

**Parashara** states: Now, the marks <note>original: "pitaka," referring to moles, spots, or pustules</note> of white, red, yellow, and black colors, according to their specific locations and colors, yield results in a sequential order. Among these, a red mark on the joints <note>likely the facial joints or temples</note> indicates the acquisition of a golden consecration <note>anointment or high honor</note>. On the head, it brings the arrival of wealth; at the hairline, it brings beauty; on the forehead, the accumulation of riches; on the eyebrows, fear of a secret; on the cheeks, a meeting with a beloved; on the eyes, grief regarding sons; and between the eyes, the sight of something desired. On the temples, it indicates renunciation <note>becoming a wandering ascetic</note>, anxiety, and the shedding of tears.

Now, as it is said: A mark residing on the tip or side of the nose brings the gain of clothing, while others say it brings fire. On the heart, chin, or palms, it brings abundant wealth. On the forehead, it indicates a rise in status. On the throat, it signifies ornaments and jewelry. 

To clarify the sequence:
*   **Side of the nose:** Gain of garments and clothes.
*   **Cheeks:** Gain of gold.
*   **Bridge of the nose:** Bestowal of boons.
*   **Chin:** Attainment of beauty and stability.
*   **Forehead:** Production of vast wealth.
*   **Throat:** The wearing of necklaces or ornaments.
*   **Corners of the eyes:** Beauty and grace.

Furthermore, a mark on the tip of the nose combined with one on the throat indicates the arrival of spiritual knowledge <note>original: "adhyatma-jnanam," knowledge of the self</note>.

**Parashara** further notes: Gain of gold on the cheeks; gain of a boon on the bridge of the nose; food and drink on the chin; auspiciousness in the center of the heart; ornaments on the head-joint and neck; but an injury by a weapon on the ears, or the hearing of spiritual truths depending on the specific form.

Now, as it is said: Marks located on the head-joint, neck, arms, and heart respectively bring injury, suffering, or the gain of offspring. On the shoulders, they may bring wandering or the destruction of success. On the armpits, they grant a multitude of riches.

To explain the sequence:
*   **Head-joint:** Injury by a weapon.
*   **Neck and arms:** Blows or distress.
*   **Heart:** Gain of a son.
*   **Eyebrows and Breasts:** Gain of children.
*   **Sides:** Both auspiciousness and grief.
*   **Chest:** Attainment of the beloved or desired objects.
*   **Shoulders:** Labor and wandering <note>original: "atana," traveling or roaming</note>.
*   **Armpits:** Either the destruction of wealth or the acquisition of many types of riches.

Now, it is also said: Marks on the back and the pair of arms destroy the effects of a collection of bad dreams. They also indicate restraint, while marks arising on the wrists and upper arms indicate the acquisition of jewelry.

It correlates specific physical locations with outcomes such as wealth, the birth of children, spiritual knowledge, or physical injury.</summary>
