# Page 163

If the second toe <note>the index toe</note> surpasses the big toe, she may be of bad character and very sinful. For a woman whose little toe or the ring toe does not touch the earth, or for one whose second toe surpasses the big toe, she is considered unchaste and sinful. 

As it is said: 
"The woman whose little toe does not touch the ground is a destroyer of her husband; if this occurs in her maidenhood, she should be designated a harlot. If the second toe exceeds the big toe in length, she will take a lover as a maiden—what then to say of her when she reaches her youth?"

### Characteristics of the Legs and Abdomen

Now the characteristics of the pair of shanks and the abdomen are described:
Those with elevated calves, veiny shanks, or shanks that are either hairless or excessively fleshy, or an abdomen that is sunken, small, or shaped like a pot, belong to the sorrowful.

"Shanks" refers to the part below the knees. "Elevated calves" refers to the back part of the leg being high or bulging. "Veiny" means covered in a network of veins. "Hairless" means lacking flesh <note>likely implying a bony appearance</note>, and "hairy" means having excessive hair. Those women who have excessively fleshy shanks or high calves are sorrowful and perpetually deprived of sons and wealth.

Regarding the "leftward whirl": If the private parts <note>original: "bhaga"</note> have hair that turns in a leftward whirl, or if it is sunken, protruding, or excessively narrow, it belongs to the sorrowful. 

As it is said: 
"She whose private parts have a leftward whirl, are long, or appear fleshless and sunken—by that defect, such a woman becomes a prostitute. Regarding the pot-shape: when the abdomen is shaped like a jar, it belongs only to the sorrowful."

As it is said: 
"A girl with a hanging belly, or one with a depleted abdomen, or a broken abdomen, is afflicted by grief and shall attain the status of a slave."

### Characteristics of the Neck

Now the characteristics of the neck are described:
With a short neck comes extreme poverty; with a long neck, the destruction of the family; with a thick neck, the woman possesses a fierce temperament.

By the neck <note>the supporter of the head</note>, if it is very short or below the standard proportion, there is poverty and lack of wealth. If it is excessively long, exceeding the proportion, there is the destruction of the lineage. With a thick neck—one that is wide and flat—the woman possesses "fierceness," meaning cruelty.

As it is said: 
"A long neck causes the destruction of the family; a short neck leads to poverty. A thick neck indicates a fierce nature; thus one should speak of a woman's thick neck."

### Characteristics of the Eyes and Cheeks

Now the characteristics of the eyes and cheeks are described:
She whose eyes are squinting or tawny is of bad character and unsteady. She whose cheeks show dimples when smiling is said to be unchaste.

For a woman whose eyes are squinting <note>original: "kekara"</note> or tawny <note>brownish-yellow</note>, she is of bad character and unrighteous. Likewise, if her gaze is unsteady and flickering like a tigress, she follows her own whims.

As it is said: 
"A girl with eyes like a pigeon, or with glass-like eyes, or with wandering and intoxicated eyes—the wise man should avoid such a maiden."

Regarding the cheeks: For a woman whose cheeks dimple during laughter...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of feminine physiognomy (Samudrika Shastra), focusing on physical traits that are considered inauspicious or indicative of a woman's character and the fate of her kinsmen.</meta>

The eyes and the hollows of the face become like pits; what is to be seen there? Such women are undoubtedly and without question considered unchaste; they speak with a heart that is far from virtuous. **And so it is said:** She whose <unclear>eyes</unclear> are equal <note>original: "sama-ja-jayante"</note> and sunken, she destroys her husband; such a woman is known among people to wander for a long time.

**And so says Parashara:** <note>Parashara is a revered Vedic seer and author of early texts on astrology and law.</note> She whose cheeks show dimples when she laughs, or whose cheeks are thick in shape or deformed, she is not auspicious. Even she whose fingers do not touch the base of the thumb <note>referring to the proportions of the palm</note>, or whose middle finger is short, should be designated as of the lowest character.

**Now, regarding the hanging parts:** One whose forehead, belly, or buttocks hang down excessively destroys her brother-in-law, her father-in-law, and her husband. One whose upper lip is covered with excessive hair is not auspicious for her husband, nor is one who is excessively tall.

If the forehead hangs down or is elongated, she destroys <note>meaning brings ill fortune or death to</note> the brother-in-law; if the belly hangs down, she destroys the father-in-law (the husband's father); if the buttocks hang down, she destroys the husband's father. **And so it is said:** Among women, she whose forehead, belly, or buttocks hang down, she destroys the men: the brother-in-law, the father-in-law, and the husband.

"Covered with excessive hair on the upper lip" means the upper lip is joined with a thick collection of hair; she is not auspicious for the husband. One who is excessively tall or of a very thin appearance is also not auspicious for the husband. **And so it is said:** A girl who is covered in hair, or who is very tall, or who is like a servant or a ghost, such a woman possesses a body of heated defects.

**Now he describes the characteristics of the breasts, ears, and teeth:** Breasts that are hairy, discolored, or raised cause distress; ears that are uneven, and teeth that are thick <gloss>certainly</gloss>, jagged, or uneven, lead to distress and theft; such a woman is known for these.

Breasts that are hairy (joined with hair), discolored (of a poor color), raised, or uneven, bring poverty <note>original: "rankam," meaning a beggar or poor person</note> in this world. **And so it is said:** She whose breasts are hairy, discolored, and covered in veins, that woman will be miserable and will always be a wanderer. Similarly, ears that are hairy, discolored, raised, or uneven, bring the same poverty.

**And so it is said:** Ears joined with veins and uneven are vessels of poverty. "Thick and jagged" refers to the teeth; teeth that are thick (very wide), jagged (long), protruding, or uneven (not matching) lead to distress. Teeth that are short and lack flesh <note>receding gums</note> lead to a tendency for theft; it means that woman becomes a thief. **And so it is said:** Thick, jagged, uneven, short-fleshed, and protruding teeth are known to give bad dreams and cause widowhood.

**Now he describes the characteristics of the hands:** With signs resembling tigers, wolves, crows, or herons; with serpents, owls, trees, or jackals; or with hands that are uneven or veiny, women become devoid of happiness and wealth.

"With hands" means the palms; "with signs resembling tigers etc." means marked with signs like tigers, wolves, crows, and so on. The wolf is a beast; the crow and heron are well-known birds; "serpents" are crawling creatures or insects; the owl is a bird. With signs similar to these, or signs of trees, or being "veiny" (covered in a network of veins), or uneven (not smooth), women [suffer].

...they become devoid of happiness and wealth, and are abandoned by those who bring prosperity. ### On the Ten Stages of Life

A woman with a protruding upper lip and hair with reddish tips is fond of quarreling. Generally, defects reside in the misshapen, whereas virtues reside where there is beauty. A woman with a protruding upper lip is marked by disease; one whose hair has reddish tips <note>original: "raktagra-kesha"</note> is always fond of strife and creates conflict. And so it is said: She who has a protruding upper lip and hair tips devoid of oiliness is always miserable and causes the downfall of her husband. Regarding the phrase "Generally in the misshapen": For the most part, defects are found in those of poor form, while in a woman whose appearance is beautiful, virtues dwell within her.

Now, the division of the body parts is described:
1. The feet along with the ankles are declared first.
2. The shanks along with the kneecaps are second.
3. The male organ and the <unclear>scrotum</unclear> are third.
4. The navel and the hips are called the fourth.
5. They call the abdomen the fifth.
6. The heart along with the breasts is the sixth.
7. The mouth is the seventh.
8. The <term>nostrils</term> <note>original: "ghona"</note> and cheeks are the eighth.
9. The eyes are the ninth.
10. The forehead and the head are the tenth.

The fruits of the stages of life <note>The "dasha" or planetary periods applied to body parts</note> are determined as auspicious or inauspicious based on whether these parts, from the feet upwards, are well-formed.

"The feet with the ankles" means the feet including the ankle joints are the first stage of life; here, the auspicious or inauspicious nature is certainly declared. The shanks with the kneecaps are the second stage. The penis and <unclear>testicles</unclear> follow these. Then the navel and hips are called the fourth stage. The sages declare the abdomen to be the fifth stage. The heart joined with the two breasts is the sixth stage. The eighth stage is said to be the cheeks and the cavities of the throat. The ninth stage consists of the eyes and the pupils. The tenth stage is the head along with the forehead.

Although this is the general meaning, in the context of the full span of life, one should calculate by assigning a twelve-year period to each stage to resolve disputes. According to the stated measure, if these body divisions—starting from the feet—are auspicious, the result of that life-stage is good; if they are inauspicious, the result is unfavorable.

This is what is meant: where a part is fleshless, crooked, or covered in prominent veins, it is declared inauspicious. The opposite is auspicious. In this order, if the limbs have inauspicious marks, that stage of life is bad; where they are auspicious, it is excellent; if they are mixed, the result is middling.

-> Thus ends the seventieth chapter, named "Characteristics of Maidens," in the Commentary on the Samhita composed by Bhattotpala. <-

### Chapter 71: Signs of Torn Garments

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of royal regalia, specifically the construction of ceremonial umbrellas, and transitions into a philosophical praise of women. The handwriting is clear, though some technical measurements require careful interpretation.</meta>

# Page 71

...meaning, adorned or decorated with jewels and gems. "Udaya" refers to the highest quality of origin. Thus says **Garga** <note>An ancient sage and authority on omens and architecture</note>:
> An umbrella marked with the patterns of swans, dogs, elephants, or peacocks, or with stars, tightly bound with silk and decorated with pearls, brings welfare and victory to a king.

Now, he describes the characteristics of umbrellas for the Crown Prince <term>Yuvraja: the heir apparent</term>, the Queen, the Commander-in-Chief <term>Senapati</term>, and the Generals:
For the Crown Prince and the Queen, as well as the Commander-in-Chief and the Generals, the staff should be five and a half cubits long. "Half-fifth" means five and a half cubits. The width should be half of five cubits, which means the width is two and a half cubits.

Now he describes the umbrellas for other royal officials:
For these officials, the top of the umbrella should be decorated with "Bands of Favor" <note>Prasada-patta: ribbons or plates gifted by the king as a mark of honor</note> and hung with garlands of jewels. For those other than the Crown Prince, a sun-shielding umbrella should be made that is well-constructed, with its top decorated with the aforementioned "Bands of Favor" and adorned with hanging strings of precious gems <unclear>...</unclear>

Now he describes the umbrellas for those who are not royal officials, such as Brahmins:
For these men, to ward off cold and heat, the umbrella should be either square or perfectly circular, fitted with a staff. For ordinary men, to ward off the cold and the sun's heat, it should be square; however, for Brahmins <note>Vipras</note>, it should be perfectly circular and fitted with a staff.

**Thus ends the seventy-first chapter, titled "Characteristics of Umbrellas," in the Commentary on the Samhita composed by Bhattotpala.**

### Chapter 72: The Praise of Women
Now the chapter on the Praise of Women is introduced. First, he speaks to demonstrate the purpose of this topic:
<margin>On the bed</margin>
> Of the entire earth, the city is the essence; in the city, the house is the essence; in the house, one specific room; in that room, the bed; and on that bed, the jewel that is a beautiful woman is the essence of all worldly existence.

When the earth is conquered, the city alone becomes the essence. Having conquered the whole earth, the city is the prize; in that city, the home is the essence; in that home, one specific area is the best; in that area, the bed and its coverings are the essence; and on that bed, a "Superior Woman"—a young woman who is like a jewel, not one made of inanimate stones—is the ultimate essence of the kingdom's existence. The meaning is that without this, everything else is without essence.

Now, he speaks further in praise of women:
> Women bring beauty to jewels; women are not merely decorated by the luster of gems. Women steal the heart even without jewels, for their limbs are the true jewels.

"Jewels" is the object here: women bring beauty to the jewels <note>i.e., the woman makes the jewelry look good, rather than the jewelry making the woman look good</note>. Women are not [merely]...

<margin>268</margin>

It is heard that among deer and other creatures, women who are not "jewels" <note>original: "aratna," meaning ordinary women or females of animals</note>—those devoid of the qualities of a true gem—steal the heart. However, true "jewels" <note>referring to the 'woman-jewel' or ideal woman</note> do not steal the heart without physical union. Young women, when embraced bodily, become the captivators of the mind.

### On the Comfort of Kings

As it is said: For kings <term>Kshitibhuja; literally 'earth-enjoyers' or protectors of the land</term> who are submerged in an ocean of misery, the only small fragment of happiness is the embrace of a beloved. These kings must conceal their inner intentions <note>original: "akara-vinirutam," hiding their facial expressions of fear or anger</note> while rising to conquer the strength of the enemy <term>Ripubala; the power of the foe</term>. They are constantly anxious, thinking of hundreds of tasks—both those accomplished and those yet to be done—which spread out like tangled branches. They must follow the dictates of their ministers <term>Niti-shastra; the science of ethics and statecraft</term> and live in constant suspicion of everything around them. For those dwelling in such a sea of sorrow, the embrace of a wife is the only drop of joy.

### On the Excellence of Women

As it is said: No other jewel has ever been created by the Lord of Creatures <term>Prajapati; the creator deity</term> that compares to women. Even when merely heard of, seen, or touched, they produce joy in men. From them come the pursuits of life: religious merit and wealth <term>Dharma and Artha</term>, as well as the happiness of having sons and the pleasures of the senses.

<margin>268</margin>
<margin>268</margin>

Therefore, women are the Goddess of Fortune <term>Lakshmi</term> within the home. They should always be honored with respect and resources <note>original: "mana-vibhavaih," through honors and wealth</note>. 

### On the Critics of Women

As it is said: Those who speak only of the faults of women, setting aside their virtues in favor of the path of renunciation <term>Vairagya; ascetic detachment</term>, are—in my mind's estimation—wicked people... <note>The text breaks off here, suggesting a defense of women against ascetic critics.</note>

# On the Praise of Women

...their [criticisms]. Those men <gloss>who, setting aside the virtues of women—that is, the virtues they actually possess—without following the path of true detachment</gloss> speak only of their faults through that door [of criticism]; in my mind's <gloss>the thought or certainty of the heart</gloss> opinion, the words of those wicked men are not words of ultimate truth. Now, he speaks in order to praise them:

"What is this great truth regarding women? Is there any fault practiced by them that is not first practiced by men? <gloss>as seen among men</gloss> Women have been cast aside [by critics], yet they are superior in virtue; so it was said by the word of Manu." <gloss>Regarding women, what—</gloss>
—other fault is there? What <gloss>subordinate or dependent fault exists that was not first practiced or seen by men? Speak the truth, even you who are detached from the world,</gloss>
that fault, such as adultery and so on, was first practiced by men, and later learned by women from their company. Whether seen or not seen by men <gloss>men</gloss>, there is no such shamelessness [in women]. Those women <gloss>women</gloss> who are cast into the path of fault are actually superior in virtue to men. On this matter, Manu, the creator of the Code of Law <term>Dharma Shastra; the foundational legal and ethical text of ancient India</term>, spoke of their preeminence <gloss>stated</gloss>.

And he says: <gloss>The following verse</gloss> "Soma <note>The Moon god</note> gave them purity; the Gandharvas <note>Celestial musicians</note> gave them auspicious speech; and Agni <note>The Fire god</note> gave them total purification. Therefore, women are like refined gold." <gloss>To those women, Soma gave</gloss>
purity and cleanliness. Here, the Gandharvas, a class of celestial beings, gave them a voice that is excellent and delightful. Agni, the consumer of oblations, gave them total <gloss>purity</gloss>. For that reason, women are most excellent, like gold; even if they come into contact with all colors [castes], they remain pure.

Now he speaks of their [inherent] sanctity: "Brahmins are pure in their feet; cows are pure on their backs; goats and horses are pure in their mouths; but women are pure in every limb." <gloss>Brahmins, the twice-born, are pure from the feet—that is, holy.</gloss> Cows <gloss>Surabhi's children</gloss> are pure on their hindquarters. Goats and horses—this pair is pure from the mouth <gloss>pure</gloss>. Women are pure and holy in all parts of their bodies.

As it is said: "Women are an incomparable means of purification; they are never defiled. Their monthly menstrual flow carries away their sins." <gloss>Women</gloss> are an incomparable and supreme means of sanctification. These women are never defiled or brought to a state of impurity. <gloss>For these women, month by month,</gloss> the rising of the menses destroys the stain of any wrong actions or sins.

As it is said: "Those households which kinswomen <gloss>sisters-in-law or women of the family</gloss>, being unhonored, curse—those houses perish utterly, as if destroyed by magic." <gloss>Kinswomen, meaning the husband's sisters and the women of the family, if unhonored or insulted, curse those houses or dwellings.</gloss>

They cry out incessantly, and those deeds are called "cries of destruction" because they are ruined; in every way, from all sides, they perish or cause destruction.

**Now he says:** "Wives or mothers are the source of birth; men are created by women. O wicked people! How can there be any good for you while you perform the censure of women?" <note>The text argues for the sanctity of women based on their role as creators of life.</note> A wife is called *jāyā* because one is "born again" (*jāyate*) through her—this is the revealed scripture (*shruti*). Whether it is the wife or the mother, the origin of men is entirely through women. The scripture says: "You are born from limb to limb, you are born from the heart; you are indeed the self named 'son,' may you live a hundred autumns." <note>A famous Vedic verse (Rig Veda/Upanishads) emphasizing that the child is a continuation of the parent's own soul.</note> One's own self is produced in the form of a son. Since the wife or the mother is the progenitor, the origin of men is always through the female. O wicked ones—this is an address to ignorant people—how can there be any auspiciousness or welfare for you who practice the reviling or blaming of those who are wives and mothers?

**Now he says:** "In the transgression of the couple, the fault is established as equal in the scriptures. Yet, only men are punished; therefore, in this matter, women are superior." <note>A remarkably progressive legal argument for the time, suggesting that while both are at fault for infidelity, the social burden falls unfairly on women, making them the "better" or more resilient party.</note> Regarding the couple (husband and wife), whatever transgression or violation of rules occurs—such as a man going to another's wife or a woman going to another man—that fault or wickedness is stated to be equal and equivalent in the *Dharmashastras* (Law Books). Just as it is for a woman, so it is for a man who frequents another's wife; the fault is common to both. Yet, men do not experience the same social stigma or punishment for the fault of adultery. For this reason, in this determination, women are considered "better" or more excellent.

<margin>Or by covering oneself with a donkey skin</margin>
**Now he speaks of the penance for transgressing against one's wife:** "By wearing a garment made of the outer hair of a donkey <insert>for six months <gloss>and</gloss></insert> and saying 'Give me alms,' one is purified of the transgression against the wife." Transgression against the wife means frequenting another woman. For that violation, one should wear a donkey skin with the hair turned outward for six months, covering the body, and go about saying "Give me alms." By speaking thus and begging, he attains purification. <note>This specific penance, the *Gardabha-vichi*, is found in ancient law codes like Manu and Yājñavalkya for specific sexual offenses.</note>

**Now he also speaks in praise of women:** "The desire for love does not depart even through a hundred old ages; in that matter, men are not turned away by strength, but by women." <term>Madanāshaya</term> <note>Literally "the seat of Cupid"</note> refers to the dwelling of lust or the desire for passion. Even through a hundred years of aging, this desire does not leave the hearts of men. Just as there was a union with a woman previously, if it does not happen now, the desire does not vanish even with a hundred-fold increase in age. Rather, in that subject of sensual enjoyment, men—even those with the strength of a hundred horses—are not turned back except by the influence of women.

The women of the noble lineage, out of modesty and because they have not been seen by others, turn back. By this, the desires of women and the importance of their virtuous character are demonstrated.

### Now he says this:

> The wicked, while slandering blameless women, display a boldness similar to that of thieves who, while fleeing, shout "Stop, thief!" to others.

The wickedness of bad people who slander blameless and sinless women is a form of audacity <note>original: "dhrishtata"</note> that is truly surprising. It is just like the situation of thieves <note>original: "chauranam"</note> who, having committed a fault and being on the run, shout "Stop, thief!" to others to divert attention. Having thus established the praise found in the Law Books <term>Dharmashastra: ancient Indian treatises on law and social conduct</term>, he now speaks of good deeds.

### The Man:

> The sweet, flattering words a man speaks to passionate women in private—he does not realize their weight until later. But women, recognizing a kindness done, embrace their husbands with commitment, even entering the fire after them.

A man <note>human male</note> speaks many "charming" or flattering words <note>original: "chatulani"</note> and sweet talk to women who are desirous of love in a secluded place during acts of intimacy. Later, even if there is a slight offense, women—because of their nature of "recognizing a good deed" <term>Sukritajña: one who remembers and is grateful for past kindness</term>—do not abandon him. Because they know the good that was done <gloss>indeed</gloss> <gloss>knowing</gloss>, they follow the man. They embrace him and, with a vow of faithfulness, enter <insert>the fire</insert>, meaning that upon the death of the husband, they follow him even unto death.

### Now he says this:

> He who enjoys the "jewel of a woman," even if he is penniless, is in my view a lord of the earth. The essence of a kingdom is but food and women; everything else is just fuel to increase the fire.

For a man in a city who possesses the enjoyment of a "jewel of a woman" <term>Striratna: a woman of superlative virtues and beauty</term>, even if he is poor or without wealth, I consider him a "Lord of the Earth" <note>a King</note>. Just as one regards a king, so is he. At that very time, even a king is only a king if he has these things. The essence of a kingdom consists of two things: food and women. Everything else—wealth and possessions—is like a pile of wood; it only serves to make the fire of desire and worldly care burn more fiercely.

### Now, to find peace in that, he says:

> A passionate woman in the prime of her youth, speaking softly and sweetly while her breasts are gently pressed in an embrace—one who possesses this has, in my opinion, the greatest joy in the three worlds.

A passionate woman <note>Kamini</note> who is in the first bloom of youth, speaking slowly, softly, and sweetly <note>original: "manda-valgu-mridu"</note> while being slowly embraced. While making soft sounds during the embrace, she raises... <unclear>ti</unclear>...

# Page 270

Having embraced such a woman, who is free from excessive pride, one finds delight and happiness is born. In that "abode of the elements" <note>the physical body or the realm of procreation</note>, he is like the Creator <note>Brahma</note> in the world of the gods. There is no intellect or wisdom equal to Love <note>Manmatha/Kamadeva</note>. It is asked: what is experienced there, having reached the world of the gods through even a small portion of austerity, knowledge, or sacrifice?

It is said: there, one is honored by gods, sages, perfected beings, and celestial singers through their service. But what happiness is found in the "abode of the elements" by relying on a woman? In that world of the gods, one is surrounded by gods, demons, humans, great sages, perfected beings, and celestial musicians. By the service and worship of those who are worthy of honor and those who perform the honoring, what greater happiness is thought to exist? Speak of it! By those servants, the worshipful are adored. Such is the fame of that world. Yet, at the time of sexual union, by embracing a woman, is there not a happiness that exists? Indeed, everything there is even greater than in the world of the gods. If so, one should consider the following regarding the world:

->**From the Creator down to the smallest insect, this entire world is bound together by the union of male and female.**<-

Even the four-faced god <note>Brahma</note>, through the play of Love, lost his composure because of a young woman. This entire world, without exception, from the Grandfather <note>Brahma</note> down to the insect, is tied and knotted together by the union of women and men. Without the union of male and female, nothing exists here. In this world, even the mighty Great God <note>Shiva</note>, despite his intensity, was made to assume four faces by a young woman. Here is the ancient legend: when the incredibly beautiful Tilottama <note>a celestial nymph created to distract demons</note> was being presented, the Lord <note>Shiva</note>, while Parvati was sitting on his lap, created four faces in the four directions so that he might look upon Tilottama's beauty without moving, despite Parvati's presence.

## 72. The Making of Fortune (Attractiveness)

Now, the "Making of Fortune" <note>Saubhagya-karana: rituals or qualities that make one beloved</note> is explained. First, the author speaks specifically regarding a fortunate man:

->**The happiness born of love is truly excellent for a fortunate man; for others, it is a mere appearance because their minds are elsewhere. Even if a woman is looking at a picture, the child she carries in her womb will resemble the man she is thinking of in her heart.**<-

For a "fortunate" man—one who is beloved by women—all happiness born of desire is of the highest quality and most fitting. That woman is his entire wealth. Because when a woman is in union with a fortunate man, she does not lead her mind elsewhere in her heart...

<meta>This page continues the philosophical discussion on the relationship between the mind, the soul, and the physical body, specifically focusing on how mental intent influences conception and the sensory process.</meta>

It is seen upon him. For the other unfortunate man, there is a separation of the mind due to anxiety, and his appearance is like one who has gone away <note>original: "gamanadabhasam"</note>, not being of noble birth because the woman’s mind is directed elsewhere. As it is said:

The mind is the root; when the mind of a woman goes toward a beloved man, whether she is far away or near, whatever man she contemplates, thinks of, or desires in her heart, she bears a form—a fetus—consistent with that man. It is said that the very self of the man is reborn in women; to demonstrate this, he says:

"Just as a branch broken from a tree and planted, or a seed sown in the earth, attains the same nature, so the self is born again in women." There is a certain <gloss>following</gloss> specific application here. Just as a branch broken from a tree and planted in the ground attains the state of being that tree, so too through the union of the self and the "field" <note>term: kshetra (field) - a philosophical term for the physical body or the material world in which the soul operates</note>, a diverse state is produced by the gods. Where it is produced by the gods <margin>vi</margin> <note>likely a fragment of 'vijnayate' - is discerned</note>, it is not merely by quality, but something similar arises; there is some distinction due to the influence of the mother. How does one who is destroyed come into being through desire? He explains:

The self is in the field; the mind is with the mind-sense, and the sense is with its object. This is the "long sequence." This is the very union <note>term: yoga - here meaning the connection or yoking of faculties</note> of the mind. Is there anything inaccessible? Wherever the mind goes, there <gloss>is not</gloss> the self <note>The gloss clarifies the soul's movement relative to the mind</note>.

The self is the "action-self" <note>original: "karmatma"</note>, taking various forms. It thinks with the mind; the mind dissolves into thought; the mind moves with the senses. There are ten senses: five senses of perception <note>term: buddhindriya - the 'knowledge-senses' used to gather information</note> and five senses of action <note>term: karmendriya - the 'action-senses' used to interact with the world</note>. Hearing, <margin>touch</margin> <note>original: "tvak" - skin/touch</note>, sight, taste, and smell are the five senses of perception. It is also said: "The feet, the organs of excretion and generation, the hands, and speech are the five. These are the five senses of action, with strength as the sixth. Hearing, touch, sight, taste, and smell are the five senses of perception, with the mind as the sixth."

Thus, the mind moves with the senses, and the sense with its own object. It moves by being covered by whatever object belongs to that sense. This is the "long sequence"—the order of its operation. The self is joined with the mind, the mind with the sense, and the sense with the object. This is the meaning of the "unending cycle." The senses of perception like hearing, and the senses of action like speech, relate to the "superior" objects: sound, touch, taste, form, and smell. Immediately after, the mind reports the object perceived by the senses to the self. Having remembered the action to be performed for the sake of the self, the mind gives the command to the senses of action, employing them in giving or discarding. In this manner, this is the "long sequence." This process is the "yoga" or connection of the mind. Is anything inaccessible to it? Rather, it goes everywhere. In whatever place the mind goes, there is the arrival of this self.

Distinguishing this further, he says: "This self, gone into the self, staying in the heart..." By the movement of the mind, through constant union, whatever...

--- Page 141 ---
<meta>This page continues Bhattotpala's commentary on Varahamihira's Brihat Samhita, concluding the chapter on "Creating Good Fortune" and beginning the chapter on "Aphrodisiacs."</meta>

Those who are energetic and self-respecting do not boast out of fear or engage in useless egoism, even when they have completed the tasks they set out to do. Such are the spirited ones. As it is said:

"He who desires to be well-liked by all people should speak of the virtues of others behind their backs. Conversely, he who speaks of the faults of others will encounter numerous troubles."

A person who desires <term>Sarvajanayam</term> <note>universal popularity or being dear to all people</note> should praise the good qualities of every single person when they are not present. However, the person who spreads stories about the faults of others will, in this world, receive many kinds of troubles from wicked people. It is known that a wise person acts with the character of a kinsman, whereas a wicked person is solely intent on the faults of others. As it is said:

"The whole world follows in the footsteps of a man who is devoted to helping everyone. The fame gained by helping even one's enemies in times of distress cannot be matched by any other auspicious deed."

One who is <term>Sarvopakaranugatah</term> <note>entirely devoted to the service of all</note> finds that the entire world, without exception, becomes devoted to helping him in return. The fame and glory one achieves by offering assistance to enemies during their calamities is not easily attained even through great merit; it requires immense virtue. The essential meaning is that one should strive to be of service to everyone.

Now, regarding the wicked person, it is said:

"Just as gold attains greater purity when heated by fire, the virtues of the good shine brighter when attacked by the wicked. The malicious man only achieves a state of further wickedness when he attempts to destroy the virtues of others."

A wicked person, even when trying to suppress the virtues of a good man, only causes those virtues to increase and become more evident, just as gold <note>original: "tagara" likely referring to a refining process or metal</note> reaches its highest purity when subjected to fire. He who wishes to destroy the virtues of another only attains the state of <term>Daurjanya</term> <note>wickedness or villainy</note>; the virtues of the noble do not perish, but rather become more radiant. Just as fire only consumes the dross, the noble man's character remains untarnished.

At the time of conception, if the <term>Shonita</term> <note>female reproductive energy/blood</note> of the woman is more abundant, the embryo becomes female. If the blood and the semen are in a state of <term>Samya</term> <note>equality or equilibrium</note>, a hermaphrodite is born. Because of this fact, one must certainly utilize treatments that increase the strength of the male seed...

### Now, the Snares of Desire are Described
The roof of a palace, the rays of the moon <note>literally "the lord of stars"</note>, wine served with blue water-lilies, a beloved woman languid with intoxication, the sound of the lute, sweet conversation, and garlands of flowers—this collection is the snare of Love.

**Commentary:** "The roof of a palace" refers to the top of the house. "Rays of the lord of stars" means moonlight. "With water-lilies" refers to wine flavored or decorated with blue lotuses. "Wine" <note>original: *madhu-madya*</note> is mentioned here as a general term for intoxicants; even for those like Brahmins who usually avoid it, it is mentioned here in the context of its effect on the mind. "Languid with intoxication" refers to a beloved woman slowed by drink. "Lute" refers to the *veena* or playing music. "Sweet conversation" refers to talk of love or erotic stories. "Solitary place" implies a secluded spot. "Garlands" refers to flower wreaths. This entire group is the "snare" or "binding rope" of Love <note>original: *Madana*, the god of love</note>. By these things, everyone is bound.

### Now, the Formula for Increasing Virility
Pyrite, honey, mercury, iron powder, chebulic myrobalan, bitumen, and the fruits of false black pepper should be used. For twenty-one days, even one burdened by old age, even an eighty-year-old, may enjoy a young woman like a youth.

**Commentary:** "Pyrite" <note>original: *Makshika dhatu*</note> refers to stone-pyrite. "Honey" is well known. "Mercury" <note>original: *Parada*</note> is the famous quicksilver. "Iron powder" refers to processed iron ash used in medicine. "Chebulic myrobalan" <note>original: *Pathya* or *Haritaki*</note> is a medicinal fruit. "Bitumen" <note>original: *Shilajatu*</note> is the famous mineral pitch. "Ghee" <note>clarified butter</note> is also included. These ingredients, excluding the pyrite, should be taken in equal parts. Having processed them with equal parts of ghee and honey, they should be made into pills. One should consume these for twenty-one days <note>literally "twenty days plus one"</note>. By using this, even one "burdened by old age," meaning an elderly person, or even an "eighty-year-old," though aged, becomes like a young man and "enjoys," meaning sports with, a young woman.

### Now, Another Recipe
He who drinks milk boiled with the roots of the *Atmagupta* plant finds that his vigor with women does not diminish. One should drink ghee prepared with black gram and small honey, followed by milk.

**Commentary:** Whosoever, desiring love, drinks milk boiled with the roots of the *Atmagupta* <note>Cowhage or *Mucuna pruriens*</note> plant, his strength does not fail in the company of women. "Milk-born ghee" refers to ghee produced by churning milk directly. Having prepared a dish with black gram and this ghee, one should consume it in proper measure, followed by the drinking of milk. Because of this "following with milk," one does not face exhaustion with women.

### Now, Another Recipe
The powder of *Vidarika*, soaked repeatedly in its own juice and dried, should be drunk with ghee, milk, and sugar by one whose women have borne children <note>implying a need for restored vitality</note>.

**Commentary:** *Vidarika* <note>Indian Kudzu or *Pueraria tuberosa*</note> is well known. Its powder should be "soaked" <note>term: <term>Bhavana</term> - a process of saturating a powder with liquid and drying it to potentiate the medicine</note> in its own juice. This means it is soaked in the juice of the *Vidarika* root until it can absorb no more, then dried in the sun, then soaked again and dried. This should be done seven times. This powder... <note>text cuts off due to margin damage</note>

# Now he describes the meeting (Sammaraṇa)

When the <term>Lagna</term><note>The ascending sign on the eastern horizon at the time of the omen.</note> is situated in the <term>Navamsha</term><note>A sub-division of a zodiac sign equal to one-ninth, or 3 degrees and 20 minutes.</note> of the Sun and is aspected from an intermediate direction, it causes [an event]. If the bird is "blazing" <note>A technical term for an omen bird that is agitated, facing the sun, or crying harshly.</note>, it indicates a strike from all sides for whoever follows in the indicated direction. Alternatively, if the Moon is also in <unclear>that</unclear> <unclear>enemy</unclear> sign, or if the Navamsha of Venus is in the Libra Navamsha of Cancer—this means when Venus is in the Lagna and aspected from an intermediate direction, or situated in an intermediate direction, and the bird cries with its face turned downwards, and that bird is "blazing" by position or "blazing" by its enemy—then it causes a "Sammaraṇa" or a sexual union. With whom? With a woman who is indicated in that direction. In that direction where the source is <unclear>located</unclear>, it is said. In the classification of women, if one asks about a maiden and so on, he [the seeker] performs the union with her. According to some: whoever follows in the indicated direction, or whoever is sent afterward in that direction, or whoever comes forward—the union is with that person.

# Another way

If the Lagna is in a masculine sign, situated in an odd <term>Tithi</term><note>A lunar day in the Hindu calendar.</note>, and the bird is in an intermediate direction and "blazing," then a man is to be spoken of. Then the meeting is of men; if it is mixed, it is a meeting with a <term>Pundaka</term><note>A person of third gender or a eunuch.</note>. The masculine signs are the six odd ones beginning with Aries: Aries, Gemini, Leo, Libra, Sagittarius, and Aquarius. The odd positions [Tithis] are the first, second <note>original: "dvitiya" - though usually even, here listed in a sequence of lunar days</note>, fifth, seventh, ninth, eleventh, thirteenth, and fifteenth. If a male bird is situated in an intermediate direction—East, South, West, or North—and is "blazing," then a meeting of men or a sudden union of males should be predicted. A union with a <term>Pundaka</term> occurs when the masculine sign-division is mixed; if one obtains a masculine sign-division on a [specific] lunar day, and a male bird or a bird designated as female is seen in an intermediate direction, then a union with a neuter person occurs.

# Another way

In this same way, if the Sun is in the bird-division of the Lagna, or is himself in the Lagna—this is the meaning—and a "blazing" bird cries out, it refers to the chief person due to the context of authority. In the masculine sign-division, everything follows the previous rules. In this manner, if the Sun (the Aditya) is in the bird-Navamsha of the Lagna, or if the Sun is in Leo, or if at that time the Sun is in the Leo-Navamsha of a sign other than the Lagna, or if the Sun is himself in the Lagna of that time and a "blazing" bird cries out, it indicates the arrival of a principal man for that purpose.

# Now he describes the rule of actions in another way

In all works being commenced, one should observe the movement from the Sun. Success will come in order; it should be stated exactly as it will happen. In all works or all deeds being commenced or performed, from the knowledge of the Sun—the Sun is the Aditya—by him...

The beginning of the inquiry starts with the instrument of the rising sign <term>Lagna-rāśi</term><note>The zodiacal sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth or question</note> from which the decanate <term>Drekkana</term><note>A 10-degree division of a zodiac sign</note> is joined. In this same manner, one should speak of the success or failure of actions. Following the sequence for the sake of correctly discerning the outcome, he now describes the knowledge of the physical form of the person who has arrived. If the Sun is in the twelfth house from the ascendant, the person is blind in the right eye; if the Moon is in the twelfth, they are blind in the left. If both are in the ascendant and aspected by malefic planets, the person is blind. If the Sun is in Leo in the ascendant, the person is a hunchback <term>Kubja</term>, or deaf, or dull-witted. If a malefic planet is in the sixth house and aspected by another malefic, there is a wound <term>Vraṇa</term> on the limb corresponding to that sign. Thus it is said: at the time of birth, one must consider the specific limb based on the sign.

Regarding the statement "blind in the right eye": if at the time of the yoga <note>astrological combination</note> the Sun <note>original: "Aditya"</note> is situated in the twelfth house from the ascendant, that man arrives appearing blind in the right eye <term>Kāṇākāra</term>. If the Moon is in the twelfth house from the ascendant, he is blind in the left. Here, if both the Sun and Moon are in the twelfth, or if they are in the ascendant and aspected by malefics—namely Mars and Saturn—the eyes are destroyed. If the ascendant is Leo and the Sun is situated there, the person is a hunchback, or lacks hearing (deaf), or is dull-witted. 

If a malefic planet is in the sixth house from the ascendant and is aspected by malefics—specifically if the Sun, Mars, or Saturn is one of these—and if that malefic is aspected by another malefic, then a wound must be predicted on the limb corresponding to that sixth house. This is determined by the "Time-Person" <term>Kāla-puruṣa</term><note>The concept of the zodiac mapped onto the human body</note> where the limbs are assigned to the signs. In this manner, all the signs, aspects, and forms mentioned at the time of birth or inquiry must be carefully <unclear>considered</unclear>. 

Furthermore, if the Moon is in the ascendant and aspected by Saturn, Mars, and the Sun, the person may be a dwarf or have a distorted body. If the Sun and Moon are in Leo in the ascendant and aspected by Mars and the Sun, they are sightless; but if aspected by benefics <term>Saumya</term>, they have beautiful eyes. Whatever malefic combinations were spoken of in the Chapter on Yogas <term>Yogādhyāya</term>, we explain them all here for the sake of knowledge. 

Now, to show the purpose of what follows, he says:
"Henceforth, for the benefit of the world, I shall declare the names of the Centers <term>Kendra</term><note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses in a horoscope</note> in their proper order, as refined and divided by the wise for the satisfaction of students."

# On the Determination of Names from the Cosmos

Following the previously discussed inquiry, we shall now describe the worlds as they are perceived by people and defined by the Jains <note>The author acknowledges both secular and Jain cosmological traditions</note>, which are famous throughout all the lands. Among these—concerning metals, gems, and living beings—there is a collection of various types of flaws and colors. This "collection" refers to the unification of the name-letters <term>Namakshara; the specific syllables that constitute a person's given name</term>. These have been accepted and established by the favored Sages, such as Narayana, Arka, Vasishtha, Parasara, Yama, and others. They have divided these into various naming forms according to the sequence of the four cardines <term>Kendras; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope</term> in every way.

Regarding the knowledge of this sequence: When the planets are situated in the Ascendant <term>Lagna; the rising sign</term> or in their own respective houses, one must look to the tenth-parts <term>Dashamshas</term> reached through their movement. From these, and by relying on the Decanates <term>Drekkana; a 10-degree division of a zodiac sign</term>, one should perform the calculation in order. 

The Ascendant is the rising point; the "Sun-situated" is the fourth house; the "Setting" is the seventh house; and the "Sky-situated" is the tenth house. In these circles, starting with the moveable signs, one observes the degrees reached by the planets. The portions of the planets—Mars, Venus, Mercury, and the others—are divided into nine parts <term>Navamsha</term>. Based on those tenth-parts situated in the Ascendant and other houses, one should know their residences, meaning their own homes or positions. This follows the sequence of the divisions of Mars and the others, specifically their own divisions <gloss>fourth</gloss> <gloss>last</gloss>.

### On the Divisions of the Alphabet

Now, concerning the divisions of the letters:
The Sages declare the "Ka-group" and others belong to Mars, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, and Saturn. The letters beginning with "Ya" are said to belong to the Moon. The vowels arising from "A" belong to the Sun.

The sequence is as follows:
*   **The Sun (Arka):** The ten vowels beginning with 'A' (A, Ā, I, Ī, U, Ū, E, Ai, O, Au).
*   **Mars (Bhauma):** The *Ka-varga* (Ka, Kha, Ga, Gha, Nga).
*   **Venus (Shukra):** The *Cha-varga* (Cha, Chha, Ja, Jha, Nya).
*   **Mercury (Budha):** The *Ta-varga* (Ṭa, Ṭha, Ḍa, Ḍha, Ṇa).
*   **Jupiter (Guru):** The *Tha-varga* (Ta, Tha, Da, Dha, Na).
*   **Saturn (Manda):** The *Pa-varga* (Pa, Pha, Ba, Bha, Ma).
*   **Moon (Chandra):** The letters beginning with 'Ya' (Ya, Ra, La, Va, Sha, Ṣha, Sa, Ha).

### On the Number of Syllables in a Name

Now, the knowledge of the number of letters in a name is explained through the signs and the Decanates <term>Drekkana</term>. 

They say that by observing the Decanate, a name has three, five, or seven letters in an **Odd Sign** <note>Aries, Gemini, Leo, etc.</note>. In an **Even Sign** <note>Taurus, Cancer, Virgo, etc.</note>, one should know the name has two, four, or six letters, increasing by two.

To clarify:
1.  If an **Odd Sign** is rising:
    *   First Decanate: 3 letters.
    *   Second Decanate: 5 letters.
    *   Third Decanate: 7 letters.
2.  If an **Even Sign** is rising:
    *   First Decanate: 2 letters.
    *   Second Decanate: 4 letters.
    *   Third Decanate: 6 letters.

<keywords>astrological naming, Namakshara, Varga, Drekkana, planetary alphabets, Sanskrit phonology, Kendras, odd and even signs</keywords>

# Page 330

The letters of a name <note>original: nāmākṣarāṇi</note> are determined by the planetary aspects <note>original: dṛṣṭi</note> and the signs located at the center. Through these, the water and other elements are considered in the sixty-four combinations arising from the planetary unions. This science is twofold: the first is based on the position of planets <note>original: sthiti-yoga</note> and the second is based on their aspects <note>original: dṛṣṭi-yoga</note>. Setting aside the positional yoga, we derive the names through the aspectual yoga, considering the relative strength and weakness of those aspects.

It is said:
*The planets look upon the third, tenth, trine (fifth and ninth), fourth, eighth, and seventh houses with a gradual increase of one quarter strength. Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars, along with the others, are progressively more powerful in their specific aspects.*
In this manner, the letters of a name are derived through the observation of these aspects.

### Knowledge of the Number of Letters via the Ninth-Division
<note>The "Ninth-Division" or Navāṃśa is a 3°20' segment of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuned predictions.</note>

Now he explains the knowledge of the number of letters in a name based on the *Vargottama* <note>A planet is Vargottama when it occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth-division chart, increasing its strength.</note>. In movable signs, if the planet is in a *Vargottama* position in the first half, there are up to four letters. For odd signs, the "doors" <note>interpretive openings for the name</note> are determined by the Brahmin-class divisions; in dual-natured signs, it follows the nature of the sign itself.

When a *Vargottama* occurs in a movable sign <note>such as Aries or Cancer</note> within the first half of the ninth-divisions, it is as stated. From the word "Brahmin," we understand this applies to odd-numbered signs. If a *Vargottama* occurs in a movable sign like Cancer <note>Note: Cancer is actually an even sign; the author may be discussing specific exceptions or overlapping rules</note>, then there are two letters. If it is in a fixed sign, based on the measurement of the aspect, if the *Vargottama* is in a fixed sign, there will be an odd number of "doors," specifically three letters in the name. Furthermore, if the *Vargottama* is in that same fixed sign under different conditions, there may be five "doors" or letters.

In dual-natured signs <note>Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces</note>, it follows the sign itself. This means: in dual-natured signs like Gemini or Sagittarius, if the *Vargottama* is in an odd-numbered division, the name will have between three and seven letters. However, in dual-natured signs that are even-numbered, such as Pisces <insert>or Virgo</insert>, if the *Vargottama* portion is reached, the name will have four or six letters, and no others.

As it was said:
*Know that there are three, seven, or five letters in an odd sign, and four or six in an even sign. Depending on which Decan <note>a 10-degree division</note> the Vargottama portion is situated in, the result is doubled.* <gloss>verse</gloss> <margin>anger</margin>

Here he mentions a specific rule:
In the "dual-bodied" signs, one should declare a double name. In a movable sign, the number of letters is determined by the qualities of the rising sign at the time of the query. In "dual-bodied" <note>dual-natured</note> signs, one should speak of two names. Whatever the measure of the ninth-divisions, multiplied by the Jupiter-factor or the rising sign at the time of the query, that is the total number of letters.

### On Conjunct Letters
Now he explains the conjunct letters <note>consonant clusters like 'kt' or 'pr'</note>. In cases of many such combinations, the experts speak of the conjunction based on the specific division <unclear>faded text</unclear> from the previous ninth-division...

# On the Conjunction of Letters in Odd and Even Signs

In the odd-numbered signs <note>Aries, Gemini, Leo, etc.</note>, one should first understand the nature of the **conjunction** <term>Samyoga: a cluster of two or more consonants without an intervening vowel</term> at the beginning of a name. Regarding the conjunction of letters, some say, in brief, that "conjunction is the immediate succession of consonants" <note>original: "halo'nantarā saṃyoga," a famous rule from Panini's grammar</note>. Some syllables are short, some are "split" <note>vihruta</note>, and some are **heavy** <term>Guru: a long syllable in prosody</term>. Some consider a conjunction to be split in a certain way; that which is prolonged is heavy. This occurs in two ways.

### Classification of Conjunctions

First, by doubling the same letter (the "own-body" conjunction):
*   **First with First:** e.g., *Kakka*, *Datta*, *Utha*.
*   **Third with Third:** e.g., *Lallayarga*, *Daddu*, *Jajja*.
*   **Fifth with Fifth:** e.g., *Lanka*, *Namra*, *Dhamma*.

Second, by combining different letters:
*   **First with Second** and **Second with Fourth:** e.g., *Khakha*, *Khadaka*.
*   **Third with Fourth:** e.g., *Vadgha*, *Daddha*, *Mriddha*.
These are all considered **heavy**.

Others are simply conjunctions, such as the Fifth letter combined with the First, Second, Third, or Fourth:
*   **Fifth with First:** e.g., *Shanka*.
*   **Fifth with Second:** e.g., *Shankha*.
*   **Fifth with Third:** e.g., *Shringam*.
*   **Fifth with Fourth:** e.g., *Sangha*.

Some scholars speak differently, saying that even "own-body" conjunctions are called "split" <note>vihruta</note>, such as *Dhamma* or *Datta*. Combinations like First-Second and Third-Fourth are defined as conjunctions and are called **heavy**. Others are merely conjunctions, such as *Pushya*, *Namah*, *Vatsa*, *Kir*, and *Smara*. 

An initial conjunction is seen in names like *Shridhar*, *Kir*, and *Smara*. In many cases, such as at the beginning of a ritual, some declare these to be conjunctions.

### Names in Even Signs

In the even-numbered signs <note>Taurus, Cancer, Virgo, etc.</note>, they say the name is "conjunction-final" <note>Samyogapara</note>. This means the conjunction follows the initial vowel. 
*   **Two-syllable names:** e.g., *Padma*, *Dhar*, *Vatsa*.
*   **Four-syllable names:** e.g., *Vasudatta*, *Devaratta*, *Vayugupta*.

The "portion of one's own sign" <note>svāṃśaka</note> is said to be "double-taken" <note>dvihuta</note>. 
For the signs **Aries, Taurus, Capricorn, and Virgo** <note>original: "ajavṛṣamṛgāṅganā"</note>, as well as Cancer and Libra, the solar portions are used. When multiplied by ten, twelve, or twenty-nine, and then divided, the result in that specific sign-portion becomes "double-taken" in a name with letters. "Double-taken" <note>dvihuta</note> means taken twice. Why? Because of the conjunction of the zodiac signs. 

Therefore, the "double-taken" letter for a portion of the sign is the letter 'A', as in *Kedara* or *Dharadhara*. Now, in an even sign, the name should have an even number of letters and be "double-taken," such as *Devadatta* or *Dharadhara*. Two-syllable examples include *Valla* and *Lakshmi*. 

The previous factor arises from the portion of the sign; the subsequent factor is distinct. By "that," the ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> is referred to. In the division of that "house" <note>astrological sign</note>, the Sun's gift... <unclear>illegible due to staining</unclear> ...here it operates through the conjunction of Capricorn and others. In an odd sign, the "odd-letter" <note>first letter</note> is the primary one. In an even sign, the "even-letter" <note>second or fourth</note> is used.

<page-num>332</page-num>

<margin>332</margin> For example: *Khuravah*, *Dharmah*, *Buddhah*. These are four-syllabled words, and so on. Now he describes another specific rule: By the application of <term>matras</term><note>vowel measures or marks</note> and the conjunction of <term>Drekkanas</term><note>a decan; a 10-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</note> in the <term>Horas</term><note>hour or half-sign</note>. When cruel planets or others are powerful in the ninth or fifth positions, the result follows accordingly. In that case, through the application of *matras*, there is a primary conjunction at the beginning. When a planet is situated there, the first union occurs through the *matras*. Just as the sequence of *Drekkanas* is well-known in the pronunciation of letters, the application of *matras* follows the sequence of the *Drekkanas*. This is what is meant: from Aries to Pisces, there is an application of *matras* to the letters of the 27 <term>Drekkanas</term><note>3 decans per sign x 9 signs mentioned here, though usually 36 total</note>. In the even *Drekkanas*, the *matras* are applied to even letters. In odd signs, for example: *Shridharah*; in even signs, for example: *Ishvarah*. Regarding "strength in the sky" <note>original: "nabho-balah"</note>, those signs which are powerful in the tenth house are called "sky-strong." As it has been said:

> Human-shaped signs are strong in the Ascendant; four-footed signs are strong in the middle of the sky <note>the 10th house</note>; water-dwelling signs are strong in the fourth house; and the scorpion <note>Scorpio</note> is strong in the seventh house.

Now, regarding the <term>Kendra</term><note>the four angular houses: 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th</note> designations... among those, according to the rules, the insects, humans, water-dwellers, and beasts are strong. Among those with "sky-strength," they indicate the <term>matra</term> of the "Dharma" <note>9th house</note> at the end, such as: *Shrivatsah*, *Vasudevah*, *Naishravanah*, *Shauduh*. In the "arm of the waters" <note>the 4th house</note>, the strength is at the beginning of the word, such as: *Saddhishranah*, *Suravatsah*. The <term>Visarga</term><note>a Sanskrit phonetic feature represented by two dots (:), sounding like a soft 'h'</note> should be known to be added when there is strength in the seventh house... as follows. Now, regarding the "Head-rising" <note>Shirshodaya</note> signs... among those, the wise should know the horizontal, long, or short vowels. "Head-rising" means rising with the head first. As it has been said:

> The human signs are Head-rising, along with the Lion <note>Leo</note> and the Scorpion <note>Scorpio</note>, as stated by the Greeks <note>original: "Yavanai"</note>. The Fish <note>Pisces</note> rises both ways, and the rest rise from the back <note>Prishtodaya</note>.

And also: Taurus, Aries, Cancer, Gemini, and Capricorn are called night-signs. Those that rise from the back, excluding Gemini, are stated to be so. The Head-rising signs <margin>33</margin> are strong during the day, while the others are strong at night, and the one that rises both ways <note>Pisces</note> is strong at twilight. Thus, in the Head-rising signs, he says there is a short *matra* at the end, such as *Udvi*. In those called "Back-rising" <note>Prishtodaya</note>, they say there is a *matra* at the end like *Da*. For the "Both-ways rising" sign, which is Pisces, the wise scholar should know that *matra* to be "horizontal" <note>perhaps referring to a specific accent or vowel length</note>.

<vocab>Drekkana (Decan), Shirshodaya (Head-rising sign), Prishtodaya (Back-rising sign), Jyotisha (Astrology), Rashi (Zodiac sign), Visarga (Aspiration mark)</vocab>

<summary>This page details the complex relationship between astrological positions (houses and signs) and the phonetic construction of names, specifically how the rising direction of a sign affects vowel length and placement.</summary>
<keywords>astrological phonetics, Drekkanas, Shirshodaya, Prishtodaya, house strength, Sanskrit grammar, zodiac signs</keywords>

--- Page 149 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological phonetics, specifically how the strength and position of planets determine the length and number of letters in a name or mantra.</meta>

As the God of Gods <note>likely referring to Shiva or the Supreme Intelligence governing the stars</note> has ordained, among the long vowels such as *Aindra* <note>the 'Ai' sound</note> and others, "long" is defined by the extension of the sound. One should recognize the long vowels as: **A, I, U, Ri, Lri, E, Ai, O, Au**. Among these, some are considered auspicious or "middle-natured." The remaining ones are short, namely: **a, i, u, ri, lri**. 

In this system, the long vowels correspond to the zodiac signs of **Leo, Virgo, Libra, and Scorpio**. The middle-length vowels correspond to **Gemini, Cancer, Sagittarius, and Capricorn**. The short vowels are situated in **Taurus, Aquarius, and Pisces**.

As it has been said:
->The measurements of the beginning and end are established by the wisdom of the child <note>perhaps referring to a fundamental or 'seed' understanding</note>. One's own positions and the middle positions are determined by the divisions of the wheel <note>the Zodiac</note> and the speed of the path.<-

Furthermore, regarding the lower subjects of the Eastern half during the *Krita Yuga* <note>the Golden Age</note>, a specific distinction is made here:

If the cruel planets <term>Krura-graha: "harsh" planets like Mars or Saturn</term> are situated in the Self <note>the 1st house/Ascendant</note>, the Water <note>the 4th house</note>, the Setting <note>the 7th house</note>, or the Sky <note>the 10th house</note>, or in their respective divisional portions <term>Navamsha: the one-ninth subdivision of a sign</term>, they affect the measure of the letters. Even if a cruel planet is simply aspecting <note>looking at</note> the trine <note>the 5th or 9th houses</note>, it influences the quantity of the syllables. The word "or" here implies various possibilities in the combination.

Another specific rule is stated:
A beneficent planet <term>Shubha-graha: "gentle" planets like Jupiter or Venus</term>, possessing great strength, grants letters according to its position and divisional portion. It is said that if such a planet is placed in a quadrant <note>houses 1, 4, 7, 10</note> or a trine <note>houses 5, 9</note>, or is in its sign of exaltation, it grants two letters or syllables in its own portion. The word "also" indicates an alternative interpretation.

Another specific rule is stated:
If the Lord of the Quadrant is weak in strength, the syllables and letters of the name will come to ruin. From that weakness, the name disappears or becomes corrupted. This occurs especially when the planet is in a weak divisional portion or is aspected by <unclear>the lord of the birds</unclear> <note>possibly a reference to a specific decan or mythological figure</note>. When the Lord is devoid of power, the measure of the letters produced from it will face destruction.

<margin>332</margin> Does one obtain the statement of origin, or rather the first of the direction-groups? What kind of exaltation is there in an odd sign? Likewise, when the desired lords are aspected <note>The term "aspected" refers to *drishti*, the astrological concept of planets "looking at" or influencing other points in the zodiac.</note> while in exaltation. He states the reason for uncertainty:

In the excellence of strength according to the position in the centers <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope</term>, this is certain. Therefore, the sequential union of the "doors" <note>The "doors" (*dvara*) here refer to the initial letters of a word or response in the oracle system</note> should be performed through the variation of combining vowel-marks and so on. "Center" refers to any of the signs situated in the fourth, seventh, or tenth houses. "This" refers to the excellence of strength of the lords of those signs according to their position. By grasping that excellence, one obtains the first [letter] through positional strength, and the second and others through aspectual strength. Having understood this, the sequential union of the "doors" (the letters) should be performed in order. Likewise, the variations of combining vowel-marks should be done—vowel-marks and so on, of which there is a variation of combination. This is what is meant: half-measures, upper-measures, and horizontal-measures <note>These refer to the specific strokes used in the Devanagari script to denote vowels like 'i', 'e', or 'o'</note> should be applied through the variation of combination. Now, the doors of the signs according to the sequence of the divisions <term>Anshaka: specifically the Navamsha or ninth-part division of a zodiac sign</term>:

There, starting from the first sign, the four points of the ascendancy, following the sequence of the first divisions, the attainment of the doors of their own groups from the eight divisions <note>original: *ashtanshakebhyah*</note> must be maintained. There, for those measured by that, starting from the first sign—the first sign being Aries—then the first four centers: Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn. By the sequence of the first nine divisions <term>Navamsha</term>, from the nine-planet divisions, the attainment of the doors of their own groups—the letters prescribed for the groups of Mars, Venus, Moon, Jupiter, Sun, and Mercury—must be determined. Now, the letters according to the sequence of the signs starting from the first:

In Aries, the letter 'Ka'; in the fourth house <note>original: *hibuka*, a Sanskritized form of the Greek *hypogeion*</note>, the letter 'Ya'; in Libra, the letter 'Ca'; in Capricorn, the letter 'Pa'. These indeed create the rising of their own divisions. There, the first ninth-division of Aries is Aries itself; its lord is Mars. Because it is the first ninth-division of Aries, from the group of Mars (the 'Ka' group), the first letter 'Ka' arrives. In the fourth house is Cancer; its lord is the Moon. Because of the rising of the first ninth-division there as well, from the group of the Moon, the first letter arrives. In the seventh house is Libra; its lord is Venus. Because it is the first division of that, from the group of Venus, the first letter, the letter 'Sha', arrives. In the tenth house is Capricorn; its lord is Saturn. Because of the rising of the first division of that, from Saturn (the 'Pa' group), the first letter 'Pa' arrives. Therefore, it is said: in Aries, 'Ka'...

The letter *Ka* is in Aries, *Dha* is in the fourth house <note>original: "Kollibuke," a term derived from the Greek 'kulindros' or 'koilon' referring to the fourth astrological house or IC</note>, *Cha* is in Libra, and *Pa* is in Capricorn. From the [consonant] group, the fifth letter becomes *Ja*. For Libra, the lord of the eighth Navamsha is Mars; from his group, the fifth letter becomes *Nga*. For Capricorn, the lord of the eighth Navamsha is Venus; from that group, the third letter becomes *La*. Therefore it is said: "In the Ascendant is *Ja*, in the <term>Hibuka</term><note>The 4th house or IC, from the Greek 'hypogeion'</note> is *Ma*, and in Capricorn is *La*." For Aries, the lord of the eighth Navamsha is Mars; from that group, the fifth letter becomes *Ka*. For Cancer, the lord of the eighth Navamsha is Saturn; from that group, the first letter becomes *Pa*. For Libra, the lord of the eighth Navamsha is Venus; from that group, the first letter becomes *Ca*. For Capricorn, the lord of the eighth Navamsha is the Sun; from that group, the third letter becomes *Ya*. For Capricorn, the lord of the eighth Navamsha is the Sun; from that group, the third letter becomes *I*. Therefore it is said: "In the Ascendant is *Ka*, in the Hibuka is *Ma*," and so on.

Now, according to the sequence: In the Ascendant is *Na*, in the Hibuka is *Ta*, in Libra is *Ka*, and in Capricorn is *Ta*. This is stated for the four <term>Movable Signs</term><note>Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn</note> and for the four starting with Taurus. For Aries, the lord of the ninth Navamsha is Jupiter; from his group, the fifth letter becomes *Ma*. In Libra, the lord of the Navamsha is Mercury; from his group, the first letter becomes *Ta*. Therefore it is said: "In the Ascendant is *Na*, in the Hibuka is *Ta*," and so on. This is what is meant. In this manner, having spoken of the four Movable signs, I shall now speak of the <term>Fixed Signs</term><note>Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, Aquarius</note>—Taurus, Gemini, and Scorpio.

Regarding this, it is said:
"In Taurus is *Tha*, in the Hibuka is *Sha*, in Scorpio is *Va*, and in Aquarius is *Ta*. The wise should apply this to the first divisions in the sequence of Fixed signs." The Fixed signs are <insert>Leo</insert> <insert>Scorpio</insert> Scorpio and Aquarius. They correspond to Capricorn, Aries, Cancer, and Libra. The first Navamsha of Taurus is [ruled by] Saturn; from his group (the *Pa*-group), the second letter becomes *Pha*. In the Navamsha of Leo, the lord is Mars; from his *Ka*-group, the second letter becomes *Kha*. The first Navamsha of Scorpio is Cancer, its lord is the Moon; from the *Ya*-group, the third letter becomes *Va*. The first Navamsha of Aquarius is Libra, its lord is Venus; it becomes the letter *Ma*. Therefore it is said: "In Taurus is *Tha*, in the Hibuka is *Pha*," and so on. Thus, for the Fixed signs, the wise scholar should apply this to the first Navamshas in the proper order.

Now, according to the sequence: In the Ascendant is *Va*, in the Hibuka is *Da*, in the [sign] of *I* is *Ba*, and in the [sign] of *Ga* is *Ga*. In Taurus is *Tha*, in the Hibuka is *Tha*.

The lord of the seventh Navamsha of Virgo is the Moon; from its group, the first letter, *Ya*, occurs. The lord of the seventh Navamsha of Sagittarius is Venus; from his group, the second letter, *Cha*, occurs. The lord of the seventh Navamsha of Pisces is Saturn; from his group, the second letter, *Pha*, occurs. Therefore, it is said: "In the first sign, *Va* is in the fourth, *Ya* and *Ma*..." and so on.

Now, the *Ishyaru*: "In the Ascendant is *Ja*, in the middle of the waters <note>the fourth house</note> is *Ya*, in the seventh is *Vi*, and in the sky <note>the tenth house</note> is *Va*." In the sign of Gemini, fear resides in the fourth, *Da* in the seventh, and *Va* in the sky.

The lord of the eighth Navamsha of Gemini is Venus; from his group, the third letter, *Ja*, occurs. The lord of the eighth Navamsha of Virgo is the Sun; from his group, the ninth letter, *Mo*, occurs...

<summary>This page details a complex system of "alphabetical astrology," where specific letters of the Sanskrit alphabet are assigned to the four cardinal houses (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th) based on the planetary ruler of a sign's Navamsha division.</summary>

<margin>335</margin> <note>Page number 335</note> For Sagittarius, the lord of the eighth <term>Navamsha</term> <note>A 1/9th division of a zodiac sign, used for fine-tuning predictions</note> is Mars. From his group, the third "door-letter" falls on the syllable *Ka*. For Pisces, the lord of the eighth Navamsha is Saturn; from his group, the third door-letter falls on *Ma*. Therefore, it is said:

> *When the Sun and Mars are in the Ascendant, or in conjunction with the Water-sign (4th house)...* <note>Original Sanskrit: "Lagne'rkakāramathāmbbu..." This verse describes the phonetic-astrological alignment.</note>

For Gemini, the lord of the ninth Navamsha is Mercury; from his group, the second door-letter falls on *Ga*. For Virgo, the lord of the ninth Navamsha is Mercury; from his group, the second door-letter falls on *Da*. For Sagittarius, the lord of the ninth Navamsha is Jupiter; from his group, the second door-letter falls on *Ya*. For Pisces, the lord of the ninth Navamsha is Jupiter; from his group, the third door-letter falls on *Da*. Therefore, it is said:

> *When the Ascendant is occupied by Ga, or the fourth house by Da...*

Here the division is explained: In this way, the combination of "doors" (letters) is determined by the specific rules of the <term>Ayanamsha</term> <note>The longitudinal difference between the tropical and sidereal zodiacs</note>. This rule is stated for all Ascendants. Some teachers desire this method to be applied as previously stated. This "alternative" method for the combination of doors and letters is described without distinction from the names of the Ayanamshas. For all Ascendants from Aries onwards, some teachers prefer this method, which is equal to the previously mentioned rule—such as "Aries corresponds to the letter *Ka*," and so on. Here, another variation is mentioned:

> *The angles, combined with their own angular divisions, are taken separately; this is the method known by the learned for the lords of the doors in sequence.*

Aries and the others are the twelve signs. They are of three types: <term>Movable</term> <note>Chara</note>, <term>Fixed</term> <note>Sthira</note>, and <term>Dual</term> <note>Dvisvabhava</note>.
*   **Movable:** Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn.
*   **Fixed:** Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, Aquarius.
*   **Dual:** Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces.

In the first four signs starting from Aries, the sequence begins from the start. From there, the four <term>Kendras</term> <note>The "angles" or pivot houses: 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th</note> occur. Now, in the Fixed signs starting from Taurus, the sequence begins from the middle, and from there the four Fixed angles occur. Now, in the Dual signs starting from Gemini, the sequence begins from the end, and from there the four Dual angles occur. The other angles are the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th. The word "or" is used to show the alternative method. The chart starting with "Aries is *Ka*" has been shown. The angles, with their own angular divisions—meaning the Navamshas situated within the four angles—are added separately. This is the rule for the "Angle-Bone" <insert>the central structure of the calculation</insert>.

<margin>On the fifteenth [page/section]</margin>

That <term>Rashi</term><note>Zodiac sign</note> starting from Aries is enjoyed by nine <term>Navamshas</term><note>A sub-division of a zodiac sign into nine equal parts, used for precision in Vedic astrology</note>. Having established one part, one should vocalize it with the risen sub-divisions. Then, that center-quadrant should be divided by two marks. This means the upward path of the three is marked as nine. Thus divided, one knows the letters associated with them. What belongs to whom? To the Lord of the sign, according to the sequence of the sub-divisions. The intention here is based on the <term>Trairashika</term><note>The Rule of Three (mathematical proportion)</note>: if nine letters are obtained by nine sub-divisions, then how many letters are obtained by these specific sub-divisions? In all four centers, the number of letters for Mars and the others is stated. By multiplying these, there are six letters. In this same way, in all the centers of Mars and the other planets, however many sub-divisions fall into whichever center, the letters of the <term>Varga</term><note>Groups/classes of consonants in the Sanskrit alphabet</note> occur in sequence. In the first sub-division, the first letter of its group; in the second, the second; in the third, the third. And so, in the four centers: in the seventh, the second; in the eighth, the third; in the ninth, the fourth; in the tenth, the fifth. In the eleventh, returning again to the first of its group; in the twelfth, the second. For the Sun and Moon, it is said: in the fourth, the fourth; in the fifth, the fifth; in the sixteenth, returning again to the first of its group; in the seventeenth, the second; in the eighteenth, the third. For the Sun and Moon, it is said: in the seventh, the seventh; in the eighth, the eighth.

<note>The text describes a complex system of "Swarodaya" or "Akshara" astrology, where the position of planets in specific 3°20' segments (Navamshas) determines which Sanskrit letter is "active" or auspicious.</note>
