<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of horary astrology (Prashna), specifically focusing on the methods for determining the names of people, objects, or foods based on the positions of planets and zodiac signs at the time of inquiry. The text is part of Bhattotpala’s commentary.</meta>

<margin>336</margin>
<margin><insert>336</insert></margin>

The ninth [house] refers to the ninth from the Moon; by returning again to the ninth from the Moon, the first letter is derived from the divisions and sub-divisions of its own strength. Now, to demonstrate the attainment [of results], he says:

-> Regarding things **thought of**, **requested**, **departed**, **lost**, **stolen**, **sexual union with a woman**, and **food**; as well as **land**, **worries**, and **groups of men**—one should indicate the first letter of the name through these. <-

**Thought of** means the mental conception of objects. **Requested** means that which is expressed through speech, such as "may this be accomplished for me." **Departed** refers to leaving or going away. **Lost** refers to something disappearing from sight. **Stolen** refers to an act of violence or theft. **Sexual union with a woman** refers to the nature of the conversation or interaction with a woman, such as her posture. **Food** refers to the specific type of meal or nourishment. In all these matters, as well as in the various appearances of faces or states encountered, one must use this knowledge. **Worry** is the mental preoccupation. **Groups of men** refers to men, and by the use of "and so on," it includes groups of men, women, and eunuchs. In these groups, one should indicate the first letter of the name.

How is this done? Since the four <term>Kendras</term><note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope, considered the pillars of a chart.</note> are involved, the sequence is shown as follows:
Starting from the **Ascendant** (1st house), one should indicate names of things **thought of**.
Starting from the **Fourth house**, one should indicate names of **women**.
Starting from the **Seventh house**, one should indicate names of **sexual union**.
Starting from the **Tenth house**, one should indicate names of **food**.
Returning again:
From the **Ascendant**, one should indicate names of **worries**.
From the **Fourth house**, one should indicate names of **Indra** <note>original: "Shakra"</note>.
From the **Seventh house**, one should indicate names of **worries**.
From the **Tenth house**, one should indicate names of **groups of men**.

<margin>336</note>

This <gloss>commentary</gloss> was composed by **Bhattotpala** in concise verses, having set aside the food prepared by others <note>This likely refers to his original scholarship rather than borrowing from others</note>.

In the same way, he describes the determination of the name for one who has arrived:
If a **Moveable** <term>Navamsha</term><note>A 1/9th division of a zodiac sign, used for fine-tuning predictions.</note> is rising, the name has two syllables. If a **Fixed** sign is rising, the name has four syllables. In **Dual** signs, it is said to be of a different nature. If the beginning of the sign is rising, it consists of five syllables.

When a **Moveable** sign is the Ascendant and a **Moveable** Navamsha is rising at that time, the name of the person who has arrived consists of two or four syllables, determined by the strength of the Ascendant and the Navamsha. Similarly, in a **Fixed** Ascendant and a **Fixed** Navamsha rising, the name has four syllables. In **Dual** signs, if they are in the Ascendant or the Navamsha, the name is "double" (compound); one name may have two syllables and the second may have five syllables. This should be understood for the three types of signs.

Now he describes the calculation of the letters of the name:
The groups of letters starting with **Ka**, **Ca**, **Ta**, **Ta**, and **Pa** are assigned respectively to **Mars**, **Venus**, **Mercury**, **Jupiter**, and **Saturn**. The eight vowels are assigned to the **Sun** and **Moon**.
The names are also compared to those of **Agni**, **Skanda**, **Vishnu**, **Indra**, **Yama**, and the **Four-faced Creator** (Brahma). One should apply these letter groups according to their own intelligence, considering any deviations in the sequence.

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of astrological name-selection (Nama-karana) based on planetary strengths and the division of the zodiac. It follows the traditional assignment of the Sanskrit alphabet to specific planets.</meta>

# On the Assignment of Letter Groups to Planets

The groups of letters starting with **Ka** <note>The Sanskrit consonants are divided into 'vargas' or groups</note> are assigned to **Mars** and the other planets in their respective order. Specifically:

*   The **Ka-group** (k, kh, g, gh, ṅ) belongs to **Mars**.
*   The **Ca-group** (c, ch, j, jh, ñ) belongs to **Venus**.
*   The **Ṭa-group** (ṭ, ṭh, ḍ, ḍh, ṇ) belongs to **Mercury**.
*   The **Ta-group** (t, th, d, dh, n) belongs to **Jupiter**.
*   The **Pa-group** (p, ph, b, bh, m) belongs to **Saturn**.
*   The **Ya-group** (y, r, l, v, ś, ṣ, s, h) belongs to the **Moon**.
*   The **Vowels** (starting with A) belong to the **Sun**.

The purpose of this system is to determine the name of a person based on the planets occupying the "centers" <term>Kendras: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a birth chart</term> or the specific ninth-part divisions <term>Navamsha</term> at the time of birth. By calculating the strength of these planetary lords, a name is produced from the corresponding letters. <margin>Knowledge</margin>

If a planet is in its "best division" <term>Vargottama: when a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth-part chart</term>, the strength of the resulting letters is increased fourfold. <unclear>Dranam</unclear> <margin>Name</margin> If it is in other favorable positions, it may be doubled or tripled. However, if a "malefic" planet <note>typically Mars, Saturn, or the Sun</note> is present without these dignities, it destroys the benefit.

### The Formation of the Name

One should construct the name by arranging the letters according to the twelve-part divisions <term>Dvadasamsha</term> of the zodiac sign. By looking at the top and bottom portions of these divisions, the name is composed. One should first consider the first part, then the fourth, then those born of the "rays" <note>referring to planetary light/influence</note>, or according to the strength of the Sun and Moon.

The letters are derived from the specific degrees the planet has traveled in a sign. For example, if the Sun is the strongest planet, the name should be a synonym for the **Sun** <note>original: "Aditya"</note>. If the Moon is strongest, a synonym for the **Moon**.
*   For **Mars**, synonyms of **Kartikeya** <note>the god of war</note>.
*   For **Mercury**, synonyms of **Vishnu**.
*   For **Jupiter** and **Venus**, synonyms of **Indra** or his wife **Shachi**.
*   For **Saturn**, synonyms of the "Four-faced one" or **Brahma**.

Using one's own intelligence, one should compose a name consisting of two, three, four, or five syllables. The rule is this: a strong planet provides the first letter, a medium-strength planet the middle, and a weak planet the final part of the name.

### On the Stages of Life

The manuscript now explains the stages of life associated with the planets:
**Infancy (nursing), Childhood, Youth, Maturity, Old Age,** and **Extreme Senility.**

These correspond to the **Moon, Mars, Mercury, Venus, Jupiter, the Sun,** and **Saturn** respectively. If the Moon is the strongest in the divisions at birth, the person's state is likened to an infant.

<summary>This page details the "Varga" system of Sanskrit phonetics where specific letter groups are ruled by planets to determine a child's name. It also links the seven planets to the progressive stages of human life, from infancy to old age.</summary>
<keywords>Astrological naming, Varga, Navamsha, planetary strength, life stages, Sanskrit phonetics, Nama-karana</keywords>

...meaning this: for Mars, childhood lasts two years; for Mercury, the state of a celibate student <note>original: "brahmacharya"</note> lasts until the tenth year; for Venus, youth lasts until the sixteenth year; for Jupiter, middle age is until fifty; for the Sun, stable old age is until eighty; and for Saturn, extreme old age is one hundred years.

With the words of Varahamihira, for those whose minds are troubled by unthought-of omens, Utpala has created this explanation to serve as a boat for crossing the ocean of meanings.

# Now, the Chapter on Ripening is being studied

At the very beginning, he explains the knowledge of the "ripening time" <note>term: "paka" - the time when a planetary influence or omen bears its fruit/result</note> for the effects produced by the planets.

> The Sun's effect ripens in half a month, the Moon's in a month, and Mars' when it is retrograde. Mercury's effect ripens from the time it becomes visible. Jupiter's takes a year, Venus' six months, and Saturn's half a year. For Rahu <note>original: "suradvisho" - enemy of the gods</note>, it is after half a year. For meteors and lightning, the effect is immediate. For a comet, it takes three months. For a "White Comet" <note>original: "shvetaketu"</note>, it is seven days. For halos, rainbows, twilight portents, and falling stars, the result manifests within seven days.

"Half a month" and so on: Whatever result, good or bad, is declared for the Sun (on a Sunday), its ripening occurs in fifteen days. For the Moon, it is "monthly," meaning its measure is a month; it happens in one month. For Mars, it is "when retrograde"; it is said that when it is retrograde, or at its rising... <note>The text here is difficult due to the stain</note> ...it ripens on the thirteenth or fourteenth day. When setting, it causes disease and misfortune. When retrograde, it causes pain from gangs of thieves. Other authorities on tradition say the ripening occurs when it enters the retrograde station. For Mercury, the ripening is "from visibility"; as long as it is seen after emerging from the solar orb, that is its period of ripening. For Jupiter, the ripening is in a year. For Venus, it is measured by "six months"; whatever result is stated for its movement through the twenty segments, its ripening is in six months. For Saturn, it is half a year. For Rahu, the enemy of the gods, <unclear>the text is obscured</unclear> the ripening is after six months. For the Sun... <unclear>the text is obscured</unclear> ...the result is in a year. For meteors and lightning, it is "immediate," meaning the ripening happens on that very same day. "In three months" refers to a comet; its fruit ripens in exactly three months. For the "White Comet," it is "seven days," meaning seven days. For a halo (around the Sun or Moon), a rainbow, twilight portents, and falling stars—for all of these, the result manifests within seven days.

# On the Timing of Portents

The appearance of flowers and fruit out of season, the "burning" of the quarters <note>a reddish, ominous glow in the sky</note>, and unnatural births among stationary or moving beings <note>plants, animals, and humans</note> indicate results within six months. There is also the reversal of cold and heat: coldness during the hot season and heat during the cold season. <unclear>...</unclear> When something naturally cold becomes hot, or something naturally hot becomes cold, or when fruit and flowers appear without their proper season, or when the quarters glow, or when there is a reversal between the stationary and the moving—such as a tree <note>stationary</note> beginning to move or a moving creature becoming fixed like a stone—and when there are abnormalities in birth, these are said to manifest their effects after six months.

**Others say:**
The performance of actions by things that do not normally act, trembling, and the appearance of unwanted or ill-omened sights, and the sounding of things that are not musical instruments, and the reversal of the flow of rivers and streams, or an excess of water <note>flooding</note>—these are seen within a year. "Actions by things that do not act" refers to when a statue or a painted figure, without a creator moving it, appears to move or act on its own; or when an action that was never done before suddenly occurs. "Ill-omened sights" refers to seeing things that are extremely unpleasant or undesirable. The reversal of the flow of lakes and rivers, or the sudden swelling of waters, is observed to bear fruit within a year.

**Others say:**
The speaking, weeping, trembling, or sweating of pillars, walls, and idols <note>images of deities</note>—these are all seen within three months <unclear>...</unclear> A "pillar" is well-known; "walls and pillars" refer to places where items are kept. The "speaking" of idols, their "weeping" (shedding of tears), "trembling" (moving), and "sweating" (moisture appearing on the surface)—all these are observed within three months. Regarding the rainbow, its effects were previously stated to last a week; if seen after a week, then the effect lasts three months.

**Others say:**
An abundance of insects, worms, mice, flies, and snakes, and the unnatural behavior of wild animals and birds, and the miraculous floating of a clod of earth <note>a lump of clay or dirt</note> in water—these are observed within three months. "Insects" and "worms" are common; "mice" and "flies" are well-known; "snakes" refer to serpents. An abundance of these, or the unnatural behavior of various species of animals and birds, or a clod of earth floating in water where it should sink—these are seen within three months.

**Others also say:**
The birth of creatures in a desert or a lonely place, the entry of wild animals into a village, the appearance of beehives on gateways or on the "Indra-flag" <note>a ceremonial festival pole</note>—these are all observed within a year or slightly more. "Desert" refers to a place without water or people; "entry" refers to wild animals coming into human settlements. A "beehive" is the dwelling of bees; "gateways" are well-known; the "Indra-flag" is the staff of Indra. All these are observed within a year or more.

**Others also say:**
The howling of jackals, the huddling together of deer, and the glowing of the quarters—these bring immediate results. Also, the sudden appearance of flowers and fruit <note>on a withered tree</note>, and the cracking of the earth or the appearance of anthills <note>in unusual places</note>. "Jackal" refers to the forest fox; "herds of deer" refers to groups of animals. These "burnings" or omens are seen within ten days. <insert>The sound of a horse neighing without being prompted...</insert>

# Page 338

The absence of sound in the heat of the sun <note>original: "ātapamavisvanatvam"</note> is seen immediately on that very day. Even for a man filled with wicked doubts, it is seen immediately. The splitting of an anthill and the appearance of fire from the earth <note>likely referring to spontaneous combustion or volcanic activity</note> are seen as the fruit of the side. And it is said elsewhere:

The falling of rain consisting of clarified butter <term>Ghee: clarified butter used in rituals</term>, oil, or fat without the presence of a fire is also seen immediately, as is the gossip of the people. "Without the presence of fire" means without a burning flame. The raining of ghee, the raining of oil, the raining of fat, the eating of the sun <note>referring to a solar eclipse</note>, and the raining of food, drink, flowers, fruits, leaves, earth, or living beings—all these are seen on that very same day. Gossip among the people <note>original: "janāpavādo"</note> is seen within half a month <note>fifteen days</note>. And it is said elsewhere:

Regarding banners, funeral pyres, sacrificial posts, and the birth of sons, or the overcoming of seeds by lotuses; some say the fruit of the banner and the arched gateway <note>toraṇa</note> is seen in seven months. The "umbrella" refers to the royal parasol. The "pyre" is the fire-altar or the sacrificial altar. The "sacrificial post" <term>Yūpa: a ritual pillar made from specific trees</term> is the post erected by sacrificers. The "seeds" are the well-known ones that sprout. For all of these, what is produced is seen in seven months, or according to some, in three and a half months. Certain sages have stated that the fruit of the banner and the gateway is seen in seven months. And so says Garga <note>an ancient sage and astronomer</note>:

-> The fruit of the altar and the sacrificial post is seen in seven months; the fruit of the sun's heat is seen in a month, and the same is true for the arched gateway. <-

And it is said elsewhere:
The friendship of those who are extreme enemies, the sound of those who have died, and the defeat of a cat or a mongoose by a mouse occurs within a month. "Extreme enemies" refers to creatures who are excessively angry toward one another, yet show affection or mutual love. The "sound of those who have died" refers to the voices of those who have passed away. The cat is the well-known feline, and the mongoose is also well-known; their "defeat" or fear caused by a mouse occurs within a month.

And it is said elsewhere:
A city of the Gandharvas <note>a mirage or "castle in the air"</note> is seen within a month, as is the unnatural change of all rituals and of gold. The falling of a banner, and the directions being clouded by dust and smoke, also have a fruit that ripens in a month. A "city of the Gandharvas" <note>original: "gandharvapuram"</note> is an illusory city in the sky. The "unnatural change" <note>original: "vaikṛtyaṃ"</note> refers to a change in the taste of things like sweets. The "change of gold" refers to gold or wood taking on a different color. The falling or breaking of a banner, the distortion of a form, and the directions being filled with dust and smoke—all these have a fruit of one month; they are seen within a month. Now, he describes the knowledge of the ripening time for the constellations beginning with Ashvini <unclear>and others</unclear>:

The constellations beginning with Ashvini have ripening periods of nine, one, eight, ten, one, six, three, and three months respectively; their fruit is immediate. For the constellations beginning with Ashvini and ending with Purva-ashadha, the ripening occurs according to the number of months. For example: an omen in the junction-star of Ashvini ripens in nine months; in Bharani, in one month; in Krittika, in eight months; in Rohini, in ten months; in Mrigashira, in one month; in Ardra, in six months; in Punarvasu, in three months; and in Pushya, in three months. These are their immediate fruits.

<meta>This page continues the commentary by Bhattotpala on the timing of portents (utpātas) and their ripening effects, transitioning from the chapter on "Ripening" to the chapter on "Actions and Qualities of Nakshatras."</meta>

In that very moment, the ripening of the Aruni <note>likely referring to a specific reddish atmospheric phenomenon or a specific omen type</note> occurs. Now, he describes the ripening of the fruits of the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term> starting from Magha: Six, six, three, half, eight, and six; in the month of Chaitra and Ashadha, the ripening is immediate for Abhijit. 

The mansion of the Fathers <note>original: "Pitrya," referring to the Nakshatra Magha</note> is Magha. For those starting with Magha, the ripening occurs in one month; for Purva-phalguni, in six months; for Uttara-phalguni, also in six months; for Hasta, in three months; for Chitra, in half a month; for Svati, in eight months; for Vishakha, in three months; for Anuradha, in six months; for Jyeshtha, in one month; for Shatabhisha, in six months; for the two Ashadhas <note>Purva-ashadha and Uttara-ashadha</note>, the ripening occurs in four months. For Abhijit, the fruit is immediate. In that very moment, the ripening of the Aruni occurs. 

Now he describes the knowledge of the ripening time for Shravana and the following mansions: Seven, eight, half, three, three, and five months respectively for the Nakshatras starting from Shravana. 

<margin>1 5 2 1</margin> 

The ripening is as follows: seven months for Shravana; eight for Dhanishta; one and a half months for Shatabhisha; three for Purva-bhadrapada; three for Uttara-bhadrapada; and five 

<margin>4 3 3 5</margin> 

for Revati. In this sequence, the ripening of the Nakshatras starting from Shravana occurs. In this Chapter on Ripening, those portents whose faults were not mentioned are described in the chapter on the six-fold ripening. Thus: by four winds, by three fires; the King of Gods <note>Indra</note> brings results in seven days or immediately; for Varuna <note>water portents</note>, no specific time is stated as it is swift. When a wonder <term>adbhuta</term> or portent is seen, one should bring it to peace and pacification by performing rituals of appeasement <term>shanti</term>.

Now, to explain how the fault of the ripening occurs, he says: If it is not seen within the stated time, it becomes even greater or double, unless it is pacified by the gift of gold, gems, or cows, or by the pacification rituals performed by the twice-born <term>Brahmins</term> according to the rules. 

If the ripening is not observed at the time mentioned, then the portent arrives with greater force in double the time. If it is not pacified by the giving of gold, the giving of jewels and precious stones, or the giving of milch cows, and similarly by the pacification rituals of the Brahmins, then it arrives with extreme intensity in twice the duration. Whatever time was stated for a particular wonder, it arrives in double that time.

Thus ends the ninety-fourth chapter, named the Chapter on Ripening, in the Commentary on the Samhita composed by Bhattotpala.

First, he describes the knowledge of the number of stars for the Nakshatras starting with Ashvini: Three, three, six, five, three, one, five, seven, five, one, one, six, three, four, four, eight, and ten. Elements... <note>The text breaks off as it begins a mnemonic list for the number of stars in each constellation.</note>

It emphasizes that if an omen's effect is delayed, it returns with double intensity unless mitigated by specific charitable gifts or religious rituals.</summary>

# The Measurement of Stars

The measurement of the stars is given as three, five, eight, and thirty-two. <note>These numbers refer to the traditional count of stars within specific lunar mansions.</note> The duration of time for the constellations beginning with Ashvini is determined by the star-count. The result—whether good or bad—is to be declared based on the number of days equal to the number of stars. Specifically, the destruction of a fever should be predicted according to these star-counts.

The text states "Fire-Qualities" and so on:
* **Three** (represented by "Fire" or "Shikhi") is the star-count for **Ashvini**.
* **Six** (represented by the "Tastes" like sweet, etc.) is for **Krittika**.
* **Five** (the "Senses" like hearing, etc.) is for **Rohini**.
* **Three** (represented by "Fire") is for **Mrigashira**.
* **One** (represented by the "Moon") is for **Ardra**.
* **Five** (the "Objects of Senses" like sound, etc.) is for **Punarvasu**.
* **Three** (the "Qualities") is for **Pushya** (Tishya).
* **Six** (the "Seasons" like Shishira, etc.) is for **Ashlesha**.
* **Five** is for **Magha**.
* **Eight** (the "Vasus" <note>A group of eight elemental deities</note>) is for **Purva Phalguni**.
* **Two** (the "Birds") is for **Uttara Phalguni**.
* **Five** is for **Vishakha**.
* **One** is for **Hasta**.
* **One** is for **Chitra**.
* **Five** (the "Elements") is for **Svati**.
* **Four** (the "Oceans") is for **Anuradha**.
* **Three** is for **Jyeshtha**.
* **Eleven** (the "Rudras") is for **Mula**.
* **Two** (the "Ashvins") is for **Purva Ashadha**.
* **Eight** (the "Vasus") is for **Uttara Ashadha**.
* **Three** is for **Shravana**.
* **Five** (the "Elements") is for **Dhanishta**.
* **One Hundred** is for **Shatabhisha**.
* **Two** each for **Purva Bhadrapada** and **Uttara Bhadrapada**.
* **Thirty-two** is for **Revati**.

In this manner, the measurement of the stars for the <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>Lunar mansions or star clusters in Indian astronomy</note> is explained. One should understand the timing of the constellations through this sequence of star-counts.

Regarding the results: Under the <unclear>Varuna</unclear> constellation, the good or bad effects—auspicious or inauspicious—manifest over a period of day-and-nights equal to the number of stars. If a fever begins, its destruction or end should be predicted after a number of days corresponding to that star-count. For example, if a fever starts under Mrigashira, one should look to the count of three. Thus, the end of a fever is spoken of in terms of days equal to the star-count.

-> ### The Deities of the Constellations <-

<insert>Now he speaks of the presiding deities of the constellations.</insert>

The Ashvins, Yama, Fire, the Lotus-Born, the Moon, the Spear-Holder, and so on, are the lords of the stars.
The lords of the <term>Nakshatras</term> in order are:
* **The Ashvins** for Ashvini.
* **Yama** <note>God of Death</note> for Bharani.
* **Fire** (Dahana) for Krittika.
* **The Lotus-Born** (Brahma) for Rohini.
* **The Moon** for Mrigashira.
* **The Spear-Holder** (Rudra/Shiva) for Ardra.
* **Aditi** <note>Mother of the Gods</note> for Punarvasu.
* **Jupiter** (Brihaspati) for Pushya (Tishya).
* **The Serpents** for Ashlesha.
* **The Ancestors** (Pitris) for Magha.
* **The Womb** (Bhaga) for Purva Phalguni.
* **Aryaman** for Uttara Phalguni.
* **The Sun** (Aditya) for Hasta.
* **Tvashtar** <note>The celestial architect</note> for Chitra.
* **The Wind** (Vayu) for Svati.
* **Indra and Agni** (Shakrani) for Vishakha.
* **Mitra** for Anuradha.
* **Indra** (Shakra) for Jyeshtha...

<summary>This page details the numerical "star-count" (Tārakāmāna) for each of the 28 lunar mansions, using these numbers to predict the duration and recovery time for illnesses like fever. It also begins a systematic list of the Vedic deities that preside over each constellation.</summary>
